# Daily Summary: 2025-03-19

### Agents Play Thousands of 3D Video Games
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13356v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongwen Xu, Xianliang Wang, Siyi Li, Tao Yu, Liang Wang, Qiang Fu, Wei Yang
- **Abstract**: We present PORTAL, a novel framework for developing artificial intelligence agents capable of playing thousands of 3D video games through language-guided policy generation. By transforming decision-making problems into language modeling tasks, our approach leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate behavior trees represented in domain-specific language (DSL). This method eliminates the computational burden associated with traditional reinforcement learning approaches while preserving strategic depth and rapid adaptability. Our framework introduces a hybrid policy structure that combines rule-based nodes with neural network components, enabling both high-level strategic reasoning and precise low-level control. A dual-feedback mechanism incorporating quantitative game metrics and vision-language model analysis facilitates iterative policy improvement at both tactical and strategic levels. The resulting policies are instantaneously deployable, human-interpretable, and capable of generalizing across diverse gaming environments. Experimental results demonstrate PORTAL's effectiveness across thousands of first-person shooter (FPS) games, showcasing significant improvements in development efficiency, policy generalization, and behavior diversity compared to traditional approaches. PORTAL represents a significant advancement in game AI development, offering a practical solution for creating sophisticated agents that can operate across thousands of commercial video games with minimal development overhead. Experiment results on the 3D video games are best viewed on https://zhongwen.one/projects/portal .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Agents Play Thousands of 3D Video Games":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PORTAL, a novel framework that enables AI agents to play thousands of 3D video games. It achieves this by transforming decision-making into language modeling tasks, leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate behavior trees represented in a Domain-Specific Language (DSL). The framework uses a hybrid policy structure that combines rule-based and neural network components for both strategic reasoning and precise control. A dual-feedback mechanism incorporating game metrics and vision-language model analysis facilitates policy improvement. The resulting policies are deployable, interpretable, and generalize well across diverse games.  Experimental results demonstrate improvements in development efficiency, policy generalization, and behavior diversity compared to traditional methods. The authors highlight the ability to generate policies offline, combine LLM reasoning with real-time performance components, and achieve cross-game generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its integrated approach. While using LLMs for game playing and behavior trees isn't entirely new, the specific combination of:

*   **Offline policy generation using LLMs and DSLs:** This addresses the real-time latency issues inherent in directly applying LLMs as actor policies.
*   **Hybrid architecture of rule-based and neural network components:**  This balances strategic reasoning with the need for responsive control.
*   **Dual-feedback mechanism (game metrics + VLM analysis):** Provides a more comprehensive and iterative policy improvement process.
*   **Emphasis on cross-game generalization across *thousands* of diverse 3D UGC games:** The scale of generalization distinguishes it from systems focused on mastering a single game or a limited set of games. This is perhaps the most significant novel claim.

However, there are points where the paper claims novelty where it may not be fully justified.  The individual components, such as LLMs, behavior trees, and even hybrid architectures, have been explored separately in game AI. The claim hinges on the specific *combination* and the *scale* of the application.

**Significance:**

The potential significance is considerable.  If the claims of generalization across thousands of UGC games hold, it addresses a major bottleneck in applying AI to dynamically changing environments. The framework simplifies AI agent development and enables adaptation to new games. The rapid deployment capabilities offer a paradigm shift in how game AI is developed and deployed. However, the significance depends heavily on the robustness and generalizability of the system. If the presented experiments and conclusions are fully validated and extended, it would be a substantial step.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper articulates a clear challenge in scaling AI to UGC game environments.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** PORTAL's architecture and workflow are described in detail.
*   **Hybrid Approach:** Leveraging the strengths of LLMs, DSLs, rule-based systems, and neural networks makes logical sense.
*   **Experimental Results:** The experiments demonstrate optimization of game metrics and benefits of VLM-based reflection. The examples, though limited due to UGC copyright constraints, show promise.
*   **Emphasis on Interpretability:**  The DSL-based representation enhances interpretability and facilitates human oversight.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Experimental Details:**  The paper lacks detailed experimental settings, hyperparameter choices, specific metrics, and baselines for comparison.  The number of games used for quantitative evaluations could be more clearly stated.  The description of the neural networks in the task nodes as "tiny" needs more elaboration – layer sizes, activation functions, etc.
*   **Generalization Claims:** The claim of generalization across "thousands" of games needs more rigorous supporting evidence. The few examples provided in the paper may not be representative of the entire UGC game ecosystem. The authors do state further internal testing has been performed, however it lacks support from the presented examples.
*   **Dependence on LLM Capabilities:** The framework relies heavily on the capabilities of LLMs, and its performance will be affected by the evolving landscape of LLMs. While the design is intended to be model agnostic the LLM choice will be relevant to a certain extent.
*   **Black box Components:** While the rules are well defined, LLMs may act as a blackbox. This could produce unexpected results.

**Justification:**

Given the potential significance but the aforementioned weaknesses, a score in the 6-8 range is appropriate. The core idea is solid, but the current implementation and validation require further, more detailed investigation. The lack of detailed experimental methodology and rigorous comparison makes the assessment difficult. The limited examples given provide a small view into the broader landscape of the presented UGC. A score of 7 is thus assigned with the caveat that further evidence demonstrating robust generalization is needed to justify a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### One-Step Residual Shifting Diffusion for Image Super-Resolution via Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13358v1)
- **Authors**: Daniil Selikhanovych, David Li, Aleksei Leonov, Nikita Gushchin, Sergei Kushneriuk, Alexander Filippov, Evgeny Burnaev, Iaroslav Koshelev, Alexander Korotin
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models for super-resolution (SR) produce high-quality visual results but require expensive computational costs. Despite the development of several methods to accelerate diffusion-based SR models, some (e.g., SinSR) fail to produce realistic perceptual details, while others (e.g., OSEDiff) may hallucinate non-existent structures. To overcome these issues, we present RSD, a new distillation method for ResShift, one of the top diffusion-based SR models. Our method is based on training the student network to produce such images that a new fake ResShift model trained on them will coincide with the teacher model. RSD achieves single-step restoration and outperforms the teacher by a large margin. We show that our distillation method can surpass the other distillation-based method for ResShift - SinSR - making it on par with state-of-the-art diffusion-based SR distillation methods. Compared to SR methods based on pre-trained text-to-image models, RSD produces competitive perceptual quality, provides images with better alignment to degraded input images, and requires fewer parameters and GPU memory. We provide experimental results on various real-world and synthetic datasets, including RealSR, RealSet65, DRealSR, ImageNet, and DIV2K.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents RSD (Residual Shifting Distillation), a new distillation method aimed at accelerating diffusion-based image super-resolution (SR) while maintaining high perceptual quality and fidelity to the input image. RSD focuses on ResShift, a diffusion model known for its good performance with a relatively low number of inference steps (NFE).  RSD trains a student network to produce images such that a "fake" ResShift model trained on those images will mimic the behavior of the original, slower teacher ResShift model. This allows for a single-step SR process, significantly reducing computational cost. The authors demonstrate that RSD outperforms the teacher model and the previous distillation method for ResShift (SinSR) in terms of perceptual quality, while achieving competitive results compared to text-to-image (T2I) based SR methods with better fidelity and lower computational resources. Experiments are conducted on various real-world and synthetic datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the specific distillation objective and its application to the ResShift architecture. The idea of distilling diffusion models to achieve single-step SR is not entirely new, as evidenced by SinSR and OSEDiff, but RSD offers a different approach, leveraging the framework of training a fake diffusion model that approximates the behavior of the ResShift teacher. The adaptation of the fake model training strategy to the diffusion SR domain is a good innovation. However, the connection with existing methods of "fake" model training should be clarified with relevant citation. The key is the proposition that allows a tractable objective to be derived.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical implications of achieving faster and more efficient diffusion-based SR. The paper tackles a major bottleneck of diffusion models: their high computational cost.  By significantly reducing the number of inference steps while preserving or even improving perceptual quality and fidelity, RSD makes diffusion-based SR more viable for real-time applications and resource-constrained environments. The competitive performance compared to computationally expensive T2I-based methods further enhances its significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Performance:**  The experimental results show a consistent improvement over the teacher model and existing distillation techniques, particularly SinSR. The method demonstrates a better balance between perceptual quality, fidelity, and computational cost.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The method requires significantly fewer parameters and GPU memory than T2I-based approaches like OSEDiff while maintaining competitive or better performance. This is a major strength for practical applications.
    *   **Fidelity:** The paper highlights the importance of fidelity to the input image, addressing a weakness of some T2I-based SR methods. RSD achieves better fidelity metrics compared to OSEDiff and SUPIR.
    * The paper includes ablation studies to assess the influence of the method's major components.
    * The method's limitation are outlined and future steps are given.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Teacher Models Considered:** Although it shows results of additional baselines in tables, the method is validated using the ResShift teacher model, which although being top-performing, can not be representative for all the different types of SR models. The proposed method might not be as effective in general diffusion SR cases and teacher models.
    *   **Clarity of Implementation Details:**  Some implementation details, especially around the "fake" ResShift model training and the choice of hyperparameters, could be more clearly explained. The trade-off between the fake resshift and the generator in overall performance is not clear. The sensitivity of the proposed distillation is not discussed.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the method works well for ResShift, the paper does not explore its generalizability to other diffusion-based SR architectures. The remark about the applicability of the distillation method to DDPM models is somewhat weak without further evidence.

* **Score justification:** The paper addresses an important problem (efficient diffusion SR) with a novel and well-performing approach. However, the reliance on the particular ResShift architecture and the areas for improvement in implementation details and theoretical justification temper the overall contribution. While the method shows significant promise, the lack of analysis on different architectures and the relatively incremental nature of the distillation objective compared to existing methods (like SinSR and OSEDiff) prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mitigating Visual Forgetting via Take-along Visual Conditioning for Multi-modal Long CoT Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13360v1)
- **Authors**: Hai-Long Sun, Zhun Sun, Houwen Peng, Han-Jia Ye
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated enhanced reasoning capabilities, evolving from Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting to advanced, product-oriented solutions like OpenAI o1. During our re-implementation of this model, we noticed that in multimodal tasks requiring visual input (e.g., geometry problems), Multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) struggle to maintain focus on the visual information, in other words, MLLMs suffer from a gradual decline in attention to visual information as reasoning progresses, causing text-over-relied outputs. To investigate this, we ablate image inputs during long-chain reasoning. Concretely, we truncate the reasoning process midway, then re-complete the reasoning process with the input image removed. We observe only a ~2% accuracy drop on MathVista's test-hard subset, revealing the model's textual outputs dominate the following reasoning process. Motivated by this, we propose Take-along Visual Conditioning (TVC), a strategy that shifts image input to critical reasoning stages and compresses redundant visual tokens via dynamic pruning. This methodology helps the model retain attention to the visual components throughout the reasoning. Our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance on average across five mathematical reasoning benchmarks (+3.4% vs previous sota), demonstrating the effectiveness of TVC in enhancing multimodal reasoning systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mitigating Visual Forgetting via Take-along Visual Conditioning for Multi-modal Long CoT Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of "visual forgetting" in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) during long-chain reasoning tasks. The authors observe that MLLMs tend to rely more on generated text and less on the initial visual input as reasoning progresses, leading to decreased performance.  They propose a novel strategy called Take-along Visual Conditioning (TVC), which dynamically re-introduces visual inputs at critical reasoning stages and compresses redundant visual tokens. TVC comprises Dynamic Visual Reaffirmation (DVR) during training and Periodic Visual Calibration (PVC) during inference. The authors demonstrate that TVC enhances the model's ability to maintain focus on visual information, resulting in state-of-the-art performance on several mathematical reasoning benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of visual forgetting is a valuable contribution. While the concept of attention drift in long sequences is known, highlighting this specifically within the multimodal context and framing it as "visual forgetting" is novel and helps focus research efforts. Re-introducing visual information dynamically to address this issue is also a good idea. However, the techniques themselves, Dynamic Visual Reaffirmation (DVR) and Periodic Visual Calibration (PVC), are relatively incremental. The DVR is essentially data augmentation with visual injection. The PVC is a combination of token compression (average pooling) and KV-cache reset - techniques that have been employed in LLMs previously, but the combination of these techniques along with image token compression in MLLM is novel.
* **Significance:** The problem addressed is significant, as MLLMs are becoming increasingly important. The TVC strategy shows promising results and demonstrates that maintaining visual attention throughout the reasoning process is crucial for achieving state-of-the-art performance on challenging multimodal reasoning tasks. The findings offer valuable insights into the limitations of current MLLMs and suggest a pathway for improving their reasoning capabilities. It is also worth noting that addressing visual forgetting is crucial in real-world applications, where MLLMs need to consider visual cues throughout extended reasoning processes (e.g., navigation, robotics).
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clearly defined problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of visual forgetting and provides a compelling motivation for addressing it.
    * **Comprehensive evaluation:** The authors conduct extensive experiments across multiple visual reasoning benchmarks.
    * **Strong empirical results:** The TVC method achieves state-of-the-art performance, demonstrating its effectiveness.
    * **Ablation studies:** Ablation studies highlight the contributions of the different components of TVC.
    * **Case Study:** The qualitative case study is very insightful into how the method re-examines the visual evidence to make the final correct decision, adding a touch of interpretability to the model.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental techniques:** As mentioned above, the technical contributions (DVR and PVC) are largely combinations of existing techniques. The underlying mechanisms for re-introducing image tokens are relatively basic. It relies heavily on existing capabilities of the underlying MLLM to re-interpret the image.
    * **Midpoint Re-activation:** The re-activation strategy seems rudimentary (midpoint). More sophisticated strategies based on uncertainty, information gain, or other metrics could be explored.
    * **Computational cost:**  The paper does not extensively address the computational overhead of repeatedly injecting visual information into the reasoning process. Adding more images to the process will always increase computational burden.
    * **Limited scope:** While the results on mathematical reasoning are impressive, the generalizability of TVC to other multimodal tasks could be further explored.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in MLLMs. The concept of visual forgetting is a compelling one and the proposed TVC strategy provides a practical approach to mitigating this problem. It will probably inspire new solutions to the problem, which might involve more sophisticated techniques to manage and interpret visual information during long-chain reasoning.
* **Justification for Score:** While the problem definition and empirical results are impressive, the limited technical novelty prevents a higher score. Also, the relatively basic re-activation strategy is another reason to give the paper a lower score.
