### On the Standard Performance Criteria for Applied Control Design: PID, MPC or Machine Learning Controller?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14379v1)
- **Authors**: Pouria Sarhadi
- **Abstract**: The traditional control theory and its application to basic and complex systems have reached an advanced level of maturity. This includes aerial, marine, and ground vehicles, as well as robotics, chemical, transportation, and electrical systems widely used in our daily lives. The emerging era of data-driven methods, Large Language Models (LLMs), and AI-based controllers does not indicate a weakness in well-established control theory. Instead, it aims to reduce dependence on models and uncertainties, address increasingly complex systems, and potentially achieve decision-making capabilities comparable to human-level performance. This revolution integrates knowledge from computer science, machine learning, biology, and classical control, producing promising algorithms that are yet to demonstrate widespread real-world applicability. Despite the maturity of control theory and the presence of various performance criteria, there is still a lack of standardised metrics for testing, evaluation, Verification and Validation ($V\&V$) of algorithms. This gap can lead to algorithms that, while optimal in certain aspects, may fall short of practical implementation, sparking debates within the literature. For a controller to succeed in real-world applications, it must satisfy three key categories of performance metrics: tracking quality, control effort (energy consumption), and robustness. This paper rather takes an applied perspective, proposing and consolidating standard performance criteria for testing and analysing control systems, intended for researchers and students. The proposed framework ensures the post-design applicability of a black-box algorithm, aligning with modern data analysis and $V\&V$ perspectives to prevent resource allocation to systems with limited impact or imprecise claims.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the lack of standardized performance criteria for evaluating control algorithms, particularly in the context of modern data-driven and AI-based controllers. It argues that while control theory is mature and various performance metrics exist, a unified framework for testing, verification, and validation (V&V) is missing, leading to potentially suboptimal real-world implementations. The paper proposes a set of standard performance criteria categorized into tracking quality, control effort/energy consumption, and robustness. It revisits operational challenges in control design (actuator/sensor dynamics, disturbances, model uncertainty, non-minimum phase systems), provides examples of how these criteria can be applied, and demonstrates their use in a simulation study comparing PID, MPC, and DDPG controllers on an autonomous underwater vehicle (AUV).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is the consolidation and application of existing, well-known performance criteria into a more structured framework for testing controllers. The individual metrics themselves (ISE, ITAE, GM, DM, etc.) are not novel. However, the paper's novelty stems from its **emphasis on a holistic perspective**, advocating for the concurrent consideration of tracking, control effort, and robustness, especially in the context of emerging data-driven control techniques.  The focus on actuator and sensor limitations is also a notable contribution often ignored by the ML community. The paper's value is in assembling this collection into an organized package to be adopted.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to bridge the gap between theoretical advances in control algorithms (including AI/ML-based methods) and their practical deployment. By providing a standardized evaluation framework, it promotes more rigorous testing and prevents premature claims about the superiority of new algorithms. This helps ensure that research efforts are directed towards solutions that are not just theoretically optimal, but also robust and efficient in real-world applications. The emphasis on robustness and control effort is particularly significant, as these are often overlooked in favor of simply minimizing tracking error. The work makes a significant contribution to the V&V process of control system design.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and Concise:** The paper is well-written and provides a clear explanation of the operational challenges in control and the proposed performance criteria.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The paper takes an applied perspective, addressing issues relevant to practicing control engineers.
    *   **Comprehensive:**  The proposed framework covers a broad range of relevant performance metrics.
    *   **Simulation Example:**  The AUV simulation provides a concrete example of how the framework can be used to compare different controllers.
    *   Addresses existing gaps. The paper directly responds to a need for V&V metrics for ML-based systems.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** The paper mainly consolidates existing knowledge and provides limited theoretical analysis.
    *   **Lack of New Theoretical Insight:** The paper provides a useful service to the community, consolidating existing knowledge and creating a testing framework; however, it does not introduce new theoretical concepts.
    *   **Simulation Limitations:** The AUV simulation, while helpful, is just one example. The paper would benefit from more extensive simulations or even real-world experiments to further validate the framework. Also, the test case only compares PID, MPC, and DDPG. This work would be enhanced with more ML control schemes.
    *   **Subjectivity:** The definition of "acceptable" performance can be subjective and dependent on the specific application. The paper could benefit from more guidance on how to set appropriate thresholds for each criterion.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can potentially influence researchers and practitioners to adopt a more holistic approach to evaluating control algorithms. It can also serve as a valuable resource for students learning about control system design.
*   **Additional comments:**

    *   It may be worthwhile to elaborate on how to normalize the suggested criteria.
    *   It is worthwhile to more thoroughly specify which ML control schemes are best used for model-free systems.
    *   The writing can be improved to more clearly call attention to the novelty of the contributions.

**Score:** 7/10

**Rationale:**

The paper is a valuable and practical contribution to the field of control engineering. Its emphasis on a holistic and unified V&V framework is timely and relevant. The paper bridges the gap between theory and practice and is well-written and easily accessible. The consolidation of existing criteria into a comprehensive framework is useful. It is also highly relevant given the current direction of the field. However, the work lacks significant theoretical novelty and the results of the simulations are limited. While the emphasis on practical application is a strength, more real-world validation would significantly increase the paper's impact. A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution despite the weaknesses related to novelty and more rigorous experimental validation.

- **Classification**: eess.SY
- **Score**: 7/10

### Good/Evil Reputation Judgment of Celebrities by LLMs via Retrieval Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14382v1)
- **Authors**: Rikuto Tsuchida, Hibiki Yokoyama, Takehito Utsuro
- **Abstract**: The purpose of this paper is to examine whether large language models (LLMs) can understand what is good and evil with respect to judging good/evil reputation of celebrities. Specifically, we first apply a large language model (namely, ChatGPT) to the task of collecting sentences that mention the target celebrity from articles about celebrities on Web pages. Next, the collected sentences are categorized based on their contents by ChatGPT, where ChatGPT assigns a category name to each of those categories. Those assigned category names are referred to as "aspects" of each celebrity. Then, by applying the framework of retrieval augmented generation (RAG), we show that the large language model is quite effective in the task of judging good/evil reputation of aspects and descriptions of each celebrity. Finally, also in terms of proving the advantages of the proposed method over existing services incorporating RAG functions, we show that the proposed method of judging good/evil of aspects/descriptions of each celebrity significantly outperform an existing service incorporating RAG functions.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Good/Evil Reputation Judgment of Celebrities by LLMs via Retrieval Augmented Generation."

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the capability of large language models (LLMs), specifically ChatGPT, to understand and judge the good/evil reputation of celebrities. The approach leverages retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). First, it gathers sentences mentioning the target celebrity from Web pages. Then, it uses ChatGPT to categorize these sentences into different aspects of the celebrity's public image. Finally, it uses RAG to judge the good/evil reputation based on these aspects and descriptions. The paper demonstrates that this RAG-based approach is effective, particularly for celebrities with past scandals, and it outperforms Microsoft Copilot in terms of both the number of aggregated aspects/descriptions and accuracy of judgment. The paper concludes that RAG improves the accuracy and allows to better guarantee their accuracy and to obtain the correct output when judging distinction between good and evil.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a method for judging the reputation of celebrities that does have *some* degree of novelty. The use of RAG with ChatGPT for this specific task, particularly with the focus on extracting and aggregating aspects from web pages, represents a novel combination. The authors' focus on celebrities with past scandals and demonstrating the method's effectiveness in these cases is also a valuable contribution.

However, the *overall* novelty is somewhat limited because of the incremental nature of the contributions.  RAG is an established technique, and the core idea of extracting information from web pages for sentiment analysis or reputation assessment is not entirely new.

**Significance:** The paper has *some* significance in the field of natural language processing and computational social science. It demonstrates a practical application of LLMs and RAG for understanding public perception and reputation management. The comparative analysis with Microsoft Copilot is also useful, providing empirical evidence of the proposed method's advantages. Furthermore, the paper reveals insights into the ability of LLMs to distinguish between good and evil, even in the context of nuanced and often controversial public figures.

However, there are also limitations. The paper's scope is limited to celebrities, and it is not immediately clear how well the method would generalize to other types of entities or topics. The evaluation is also primarily focused on a relatively small set of Japanese and Western celebrities.  A more comprehensive evaluation across different domains and cultures would be necessary to establish the generalizability of the findings. The analysis mainly focuses on the comparison with Yokoyama et al. (2024) and Microsoft Copilot.

**Strengths:**

*   Clear and well-defined research question.
*   Novel combination of RAG and aspect-based analysis.
*   Empirical evaluation demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed method.
*   Valuable insights into the capabilities of LLMs for reputation assessment.
*   Focus on celebrities with past scandals, showcasing the robustness of the method.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Limited generalizability beyond celebrities.
*   Relatively small and culturally specific evaluation dataset.
*   Incremental nature of novelty, building upon existing RAG techniques.
*   Limited investigation of the impact of different RAG parameters or LLM architectures.
*   The prompt engineering details, while mentioned, should be thoroughly documented in the Appendix.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **6**.

The work is technically sound and makes a novel contribution but is based on established methods. This makes it an incremental rather than a foundational step. While the celebrity domain is of interest, this somewhat limits the broad scope of the results, requiring the need for further validations in different contexts.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### How much do LLMs learn from negative examples?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14391v1)
- **Authors**: Shadi Hamdan, Deniz Yuret
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) undergo a three-phase training process: unsupervised pre-training, supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and learning from human feedback (RLHF/DPO). Notably, it is during the final phase that these models are exposed to negative examples -- incorrect, rejected, or suboptimal responses to queries. This paper delves into the role of negative examples in the training of LLMs, using a likelihood-ratio (Likra) model on multiple-choice question answering benchmarks to precisely manage the influence and the volume of negative examples. Our findings reveal three key insights: (1) During a critical phase in training, Likra with negative examples demonstrates a significantly larger improvement per training example compared to SFT using only positive examples. This leads to a sharp jump in the learning curve for Likra unlike the smooth and gradual improvement of SFT; (2) negative examples that are plausible but incorrect (near-misses) exert a greater influence; and (3) while training with positive examples fails to significantly decrease the likelihood of plausible but incorrect answers, training with negative examples more accurately identifies them. These results indicate a potentially significant role for negative examples in improving accuracy and reducing hallucinations for LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the role of negative examples in training Large Language Models (LLMs). Using a likelihood-ratio (Likra) model on multiple-choice question answering benchmarks, the authors demonstrate that negative examples, specifically plausible but incorrect answers ("near-misses"), have a disproportionately large positive impact on accuracy compared to positive examples alone. Their findings suggest that training with negative examples leads to a sharp jump in the learning curve, improves the model's ability to distinguish correct answers from plausible but incorrect ones, and potentially contributes to reducing hallucinations in LLMs.  The Likra model contrasts a positive head trained on correct answers with a negative head trained on incorrect answers.  The study also explores the impact of varying the ratio of positive and negative examples, and the weight of the negative head during inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The investigation into the impact of negative examples on LLMs is not entirely new.  Techniques like contrastive learning and hard negative mining have explored similar themes in other areas of machine learning. However, applying these concepts in a controlled and explicit manner to the training of LLMs, particularly in the context of the three-phase training pipeline (pre-training, SFT, RLHF/DPO) is relatively novel. The construction of the Likra model to isolate and quantify the impact of negative examples is a key strength in the paper.

**Significance:** The paper's findings have important implications for improving the accuracy and reducing hallucinations in LLMs. The observation that near-miss negative examples are particularly effective highlights the need for carefully curating negative training datasets. The results suggest that negative examples can act as a catalyst, unlocking latent knowledge that already exists in the pre-trained model. This could lead to more efficient training strategies that prioritize the inclusion of high-quality negative examples. The ability to reduce hallucinations would be particularly important, and negative training is known to reduce unwanted or repetitive outputs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Research Question:** The paper directly addresses a specific and relevant question regarding the role of negative examples.
*   **Controlled Experiments:** The use of the Likra model allows for precise control over the number and type of negative examples used during training.
*   **Empirical Evidence:** The paper presents compelling empirical evidence to support its claims, including learning curves and ablation studies.
*   **Analysis of Plausibility:** The investigation into the impact of plausible vs. irrelevant negative examples is particularly insightful.
*   **Domain Independence:** The experiments are performed on different benchmarks (ARC, HellaSwag) indicating potential domain independence of the results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generality:** While the results are encouraging, the experiments are primarily focused on multiple-choice question answering tasks.  It is unclear how well these findings would generalize to other types of LLM applications, such as open-ended text generation. The likelihood ratio approach in particular would be challenging in generative scenarios.
*   **Simulated Negative Examples:** The method of generating negative examples is relatively simple (randomly choosing an incorrect answer). Exploring more sophisticated methods of generating negative examples (e.g., using adversarial techniques) could further enhance the model's performance.
*   **Base Model Dependency:** While multiple benchmarks were tested, the primary experiments are performed using a specific base model (Mistral-7B-v0.1). It would be valuable to investigate the impact of negative examples on other LLM architectures.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Grounding:** While the paper provides empirical evidence, it lacks a strong theoretical framework to explain why negative examples have such a significant impact.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM training. The findings suggest that negative examples should be given greater consideration during the SFT and RLHF/DPO phases. The paper could inspire the development of new techniques for generating and selecting high-quality negative examples, as well as new training algorithms that effectively leverage negative feedback.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper provides a novel investigation of the impact of negative examples on LLMs, presenting substantial empirical evidence through carefully designed experiments. The findings, while limited to multiple-choice question answering, indicate that negative examples, specifically near-miss negative examples, can drastically boost performance during LLM fine-tuning.  This demonstrates potential significance to improve factual accuracy and reduce hallucinations in LLMs. However, the paper is somewhat limited by the lack of broader applicability, reliance on random answer assignment for creating negative examples, and the absence of a comprehensive theoretical underpinning. This work can significantly influence future LLM development by emphasizing negative examples, but more experimentation and analysis are required for it to merit a higher evaluation.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### From "Hallucination" to "Suture": Insights from Language Philosophy to Enhance Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14392v1)
- **Authors**: Qiantong Wang
- **Abstract**: This paper explores hallucination phenomena in large language models (LLMs) through the lens of language philosophy and psychoanalysis. By incorporating Lacan's concepts of the "chain of signifiers" and "suture points," we propose the Anchor-RAG framework as a novel approach to mitigate hallucinations. In contrast to the predominant reliance on trial-and-error experiments, constant adjustments of mathematical formulas, or resource-intensive methods that emphasize quantity over quality, our approach returns to the fundamental principles of linguistics to analyze the root causes of hallucinations in LLMs. Drawing from robust theoretical foundations, we derive algorithms and models that are not only effective in reducing hallucinations but also enhance LLM performance and improve output quality. This paper seeks to establish a comprehensive theoretical framework for understanding hallucinations in LLMs and aims to challenge the prevalent "guess-and-test" approach and rat race mentality in the field. We aspire to pave the way for a new era of interpretable LLMs, offering deeper insights into the inner workings of language-based AI systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach called Anchor-RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) to mitigate hallucinations in large language models (LLMs). It draws inspiration from Lacanian language philosophy, specifically the concepts of "chain of signifiers" and "quilting points."  The Anchor-RAG framework aims to identify these "quilting points" (or "anchors") in input prompts, retrieve relevant context using these anchors as query terms, and then generate responses grounded in this context.  The paper argues that this grounding in relevant information helps to reduce ambiguity and therefore hallucinations. It introduces the framework's key steps: Anchor Identification, Contextual Retrieval, and Controlled Generation. While the paper outlines the theoretical framework and planned experimental setup, it does not yet present any empirical results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of Lacanian language philosophy to the problem of hallucination in LLMs.  Most current approaches rely on empirical methods (trial and error, scaling up data, tweaking models) rather than a theoretical understanding of why hallucinations occur.  Connecting the abstract concepts of psychoanalysis to a concrete engineering problem in NLP is a significant, albeit high-risk, departure from the norm.

* **Strengths:**

    * **Novelty of Approach:** Applying Lacanian philosophy to LLMs is a genuinely novel idea, offering a new perspective on a well-studied problem.
    * **Theoretical Grounding:** The paper attempts to provide a more fundamental, theoretically-driven explanation for the cause of hallucinations, rather than relying solely on empirical observations. This is a valuable contribution.
    * **Potential for Interpretability:** Identifying "anchor points" could potentially lead to more interpretable LLM behavior, allowing researchers to understand *why* certain outputs are generated.
    * **Addressing a core issue:** By adressing the source of Hallucinations by using Anchor-RAG, the solution has the possibility to greatly impact the outcome of LLM models.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Lack of Empirical Validation:**  The most significant weakness is the absence of any experimental results.  The paper describes the planned experimental setup, but without data, it's impossible to assess the actual effectiveness of the Anchor-RAG framework. The theoretical arguments, while interesting, remain speculative until supported by evidence.
    * **Complexity and Abstraction:**  Lacanian philosophy is notoriously complex and can be difficult to understand and operationalize. The paper provides a basic introduction, but the connection between these abstract concepts and the specific algorithms of Anchor-RAG might not be immediately clear to all readers. The "anchor" is not clearly defined and its functionality not immediately understandable.
    * **Potential Over-Simplification:** There is a risk of over-simplifying complex philosophical concepts to fit the NLP context. The analogy between the "unconscious subject" and the LLM context, for example, may be seen as a stretch.
    * **Limited Scope:** The current paper focuses mainly on the theoretical framework and planned experiments. Optimization strategies are touched upon, but further detailed analysis might be crucial for practical viability.

* **Significance:**

    * If the Anchor-RAG framework proves effective, it could represent a significant step towards more reliable and interpretable LLMs. The philosophical grounding could open new avenues for research into the nature of language understanding and AI.
    * Even if the framework doesn't yield dramatic improvements in performance, the paper could still be valuable for prompting other researchers to consider alternative theoretical perspectives when addressing the challenges of LLMs. The philosophical point of view could be helpful.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the lack of empirical results, the paper's score is significantly limited.  While the novelty of the approach and the attempt to provide a deeper theoretical foundation are commendable, without experimental validation, the significance is currently speculative.

Score: 5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a highly innovative idea with the potential to significantly impact the field. However, the absence of experimental results and the inherent complexity of applying abstract philosophical concepts to a practical problem necessitate a moderate score. The idea is high-risk, high-reward. A lower score wouldn't recognize the interesting theoretical contribution, while a higher score would be inappropriate without empirical support. A score of 5 reflects the potential while acknowledging the current limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### Large Language Models for Virtual Human Gesture Selection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14408v1)
- **Authors**: Parisa Ghanad Torshizi, Laura B. Hensel, Ari Shapiro, Stacy C. Marsella
- **Abstract**: Co-speech gestures convey a wide variety of meanings and play an important role in face-to-face human interactions. These gestures significantly influence the addressee's engagement, recall, comprehension, and attitudes toward the speaker. Similarly, they impact interactions between humans and embodied virtual agents. The process of selecting and animating meaningful gestures has thus become a key focus in the design of these agents. However, automating this gesture selection process poses a significant challenge. Prior gesture generation techniques have varied from fully automated, data-driven methods, which often struggle to produce contextually meaningful gestures, to more manual approaches that require crafting specific gesture expertise and are time-consuming and lack generalizability. In this paper, we leverage the semantic capabilities of Large Language Models to develop a gesture selection approach that suggests meaningful, appropriate co-speech gestures. We first describe how information on gestures is encoded into GPT-4. Then, we conduct a study to evaluate alternative prompting approaches for their ability to select meaningful, contextually relevant gestures and to align them appropriately with the co-speech utterance. Finally, we detail and demonstrate how this approach has been implemented within a virtual agent system, automating the selection and subsequent animation of the selected gestures for enhanced human-agent interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4, for automated gesture selection in virtual human interactions. It addresses the challenge of generating meaningful and contextually appropriate co-speech gestures, which are crucial for engaging human-agent interactions. The authors investigate different prompting strategies for GPT-4 to select suitable gestures and determine when to gesture. The approach combines LLM-driven discourse analysis (rheme/theme identification) with a pre-defined repertoire of gestures to guide the selection process.  They implement this approach within a virtual human architecture and evaluate its performance in selecting appropriate gestures, inference speed, and alignment with actual human gesturing.  They find that prompts incorporating gesture intents and examples yield the most appropriate gestures, and while GPT-4 is promising, its inference time needs improvement for real-time applications. The integration with the SIMA framework and preliminary exploration of spatial consistency in ideational units are also described.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The use of LLMs for gesture selection is not entirely new (building on previous work), but the paper makes several incremental contributions. The systematic evaluation of different prompting strategies, the attempt to automate "when to gesture" by rheme/theme analysis, and the exploration of spatial relationships in ideational units elevate the work beyond a simple LLM application. The integration into a full virtual human architecture and workflow is a valuable contribution, showing practical applicability.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a core challenge in embodied agent research: generating believable and effective nonverbal behavior. Automating gesture selection has the potential to make virtual humans more engaging, persuasive, and communicative. The findings related to prompt engineering offer practical guidance for researchers seeking to leverage LLMs for this purpose. The identified limitations regarding latency highlight areas for future research and the need for specialized or fine-tuned LLMs for real-time gesture generation. The preliminary exploration of ideational unit handling, while requiring more investigation, points toward future directions.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear research questions and a well-defined experimental design.
    *   Systematic evaluation of different prompting approaches with both quantitative and qualitative analysis.
    *   Practical implementation within a virtual human architecture (SIMA), demonstrating real-world applicability.
    *   Consideration of both "what gesture" and "when to gesture" aspects.
    *   Acknowledged limitations and identified avenues for future research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on GPT-4's API introduces potential cost and scalability concerns. Fine-tuning smaller, faster models would address this.
    *   The dataset used for ground truth is limited, derived from a single speaker. Expanding the dataset would improve generalizability.
    *   The analysis of ideational units and spatial consistency is preliminary and requires more thorough evaluation.
    *   The analysis of gesture types could be more sophisticated, moving beyond broad categories like "metaphoric."

*   **Overall:** The paper represents a solid contribution to the field by systematically exploring the use of LLMs for gesture selection in virtual humans, especially as a component in a system. It moves beyond proof-of-concept and provides concrete insights into the strengths and weaknesses of this approach. The identified limitations point to important areas for further research.
*Score: 7.5*

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unifying Text Semantics and Graph Structures for Temporal Text-attributed Graphs with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14411v1)
- **Authors**: Siwei Zhang, Yun Xiong, Yateng Tang, Xi Chen, Zian Jia, Zehao Gu, Jiarong Xu, Jiawei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Temporal graph neural networks (TGNNs) have shown remarkable performance in temporal graph modeling. However, real-world temporal graphs often possess rich textual information, giving rise to temporal text-attributed graphs (TTAGs). Such combination of dynamic text semantics and evolving graph structures introduces heightened complexity. Existing TGNNs embed texts statically and rely heavily on encoding mechanisms that biasedly prioritize structural information, overlooking the temporal evolution of text semantics and the essential interplay between semantics and structures for synergistic reinforcement. To tackle these issues, we present \textbf{{Cross}}, a novel framework that seamlessly extends existing TGNNs for TTAG modeling. The key idea is to employ the advanced large language models (LLMs) to extract the dynamic semantics in text space and then generate expressive representations unifying both semantics and structures. Specifically, we propose a Temporal Semantics Extractor in the {Cross} framework, which empowers the LLM to offer the temporal semantic understanding of node's evolving contexts of textual neighborhoods, facilitating semantic dynamics. Subsequently, we introduce the Semantic-structural Co-encoder, which collaborates with the above Extractor for synthesizing illuminating representations by jointly considering both semantic and structural information while encouraging their mutual reinforcement. Extensive experimental results on four public datasets and one practical industrial dataset demonstrate {Cross}'s significant effectiveness and robustness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Unifying Text Semantics and Graph Structures for Temporal Text-attributed Graphs with Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CROSS, a novel framework designed to improve Temporal Graph Neural Networks (TGNNs) for Temporal Text-Attributed Graphs (TTAGs). The core idea is to leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) to explicitly extract and integrate dynamic text semantics alongside evolving graph structures. CROSS comprises two key components: a Temporal Semantics Extractor, which uses LLMs to dynamically understand the evolving textual contexts of nodes' neighborhoods, and a Semantic-structural Co-encoder, which unifies semantic and structural information for mutually reinforcing representations. The authors conduct extensive experiments on multiple datasets, demonstrating CROSS's effectiveness and robustness in boosting existing TGNNs' performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper tackles a relatively underexplored area – the synergistic combination of dynamic text semantics and graph structures in TTAGs. While existing TGNNs handle temporal graphs and LLMs are increasingly used for graph-related tasks, the specific architecture and training methodology for extracting and integrating dynamic node-level semantic information via LLMs, within a TTAG framework, constitutes a novel contribution. The Temporal Semantics Extractor and Semantic-structural Co-encoder are specifically designed for TTAGs and represent a novel approach compared to static text embedding or simple concatenation techniques. The idea of using an LLM to create a temporally-aware summary of a node's neighborhood to capture its evolving context is a strong point.

*   **Significance/Impact:** The paper's significance lies in addressing a critical limitation of current TGNNs – their neglect of dynamic text semantics in TTAGs. By explicitly incorporating the temporal evolution of text semantics and encouraging mutual reinforcement between semantics and structure, the CROSS framework promises to unlock better performance and robustness in TTAG modeling. The demonstrated performance gains across diverse datasets, including a real-world industrial dataset, support this claim. The framework's TGNN-agnostic nature, enabling it to enhance multiple existing architectures, is also a significant advantage. The robustness study further strengthens the paper by empirically demonstrating that the method is less susceptible to noisy graph structures compared to standard TGNNS.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges in TTAG modeling and the limitations of existing TGNNs.
    *   **Well-designed framework:** The CROSS framework is logically structured and well-explained, with a detailed description of each component.
    *   **Strong empirical validation:** The extensive experiments on multiple datasets and tasks demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed approach.
    *   **TGNN-agnostic design:** CROSS can be applied to enhance existing TGNNs, increasing its potential for broader adoption.
    *   **Cost Analysis:** The inclusion of a cost analysis that clearly reveals how cost can be minimized for large language models is beneficial.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** While well-explained, the framework introduces additional complexity compared to standard TGNNs. Practical deployment might require careful tuning and optimization.
    *   **LLM Dependency:** The framework relies heavily on LLMs, which can be computationally expensive and might introduce biases inherent in the LLMs. The dependence on a single (albeit strong) LLM (DeepSeek-v2 in most experiments) somewhat limits the generality of the conclusions. While multiple LLMs are evaluated later, the primary results focus on DeepSeek-v2.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Specific Datasets:** While multiple datasets are evaluated, there are not as many analyses of the specific data and how it interacts with the method. For example, more information on the Industrial Dataset might help in the discussion.

*   **Justification for the score:** Overall, the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to TTAG modeling. It effectively addresses a critical gap in existing methods and provides strong empirical evidence to support its claims. While the complexity and LLM dependency are valid concerns, the benefits in terms of performance and robustness outweigh these drawbacks. The proposed framework addresses these limitations in a practical manner while achieving significant improvement in both link prediction and node classification. The method itself is novel, and the evaluation justifies its relevance.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### MagicComp: Training-free Dual-Phase Refinement for Compositional Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14428v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyu Zhang, Yufan Deng, Shenghai Yuan, Peng Jin, Zesen Cheng, Yian Zhao, Chang Liu, Jie Chen
- **Abstract**: Text-to-video (T2V) generation has made significant strides with diffusion models. However, existing methods still struggle with accurately binding attributes, determining spatial relationships, and capturing complex action interactions between multiple subjects. To address these limitations, we propose MagicComp, a training-free method that enhances compositional T2V generation through dual-phase refinement. Specifically, (1) During the Conditioning Stage: We introduce the Semantic Anchor Disambiguation to reinforces subject-specific semantics and resolve inter-subject ambiguity by progressively injecting the directional vectors of semantic anchors into original text embedding; (2) During the Denoising Stage: We propose Dynamic Layout Fusion Attention, which integrates grounding priors and model-adaptive spatial perception to flexibly bind subjects to their spatiotemporal regions through masked attention modulation. Furthermore, MagicComp is a model-agnostic and versatile approach, which can be seamlessly integrated into existing T2V architectures. Extensive experiments on T2V-CompBench and VBench demonstrate that MagicComp outperforms state-of-the-art methods, highlighting its potential for applications such as complex prompt-based and trajectory-controllable video generation. Project page: https://hong-yu-zhang.github.io/MagicComp-Page/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MagicComp paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MagicComp, a training-free framework designed to improve compositional text-to-video (T2V) generation. It tackles issues such as semantic leakage between subjects in a prompt and ensuring accurate spatial-temporal binding of attributes to those subjects. MagicComp operates through a dual-phase refinement strategy. The first phase, "Semantic Anchor Disambiguation" (SAD), addresses semantic ambiguity by refining text embeddings based on independently encoded subject anchors. The second phase, "Dynamic Layout Fusion Attention" (DLFA), integrates LLM-generated layout priors with model-adaptive attention masks during denoising to achieve precise spatio-temporal binding of subjects and their attributes. The framework is model-agnostic and demonstrably improves performance on existing T2V architectures like CogVideoX and VideoCrafter2, evaluated on benchmarks like T2V-CompBench and VBench. The authors highlight its applications in complex prompt-based and trajectory-controllable video generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of a dual-phase refinement approach, specifically designed for compositional T2V generation, shows decent novelty. The SAD module is innovative in addressing semantic confusion by using anchor embeddings and directional vectors, although token merging approaches [Hu et al., 2024] also exist. The DLFA module builds on layout-based attention, but it is presented as more adaptive through LLM layout priors. Given the prior work, the novelty is significant but *not revolutionary*.

*   **Significance:** Compositional video generation is a challenging problem with ongoing research efforts. The fact that MagicComp is training-free is a significant advantage, because it sidesteps computational overhead associated with fine-tuning approaches. The reported results on T2V-CompBench and VBench, demonstrably outperforming existing models, underscores the paper's potential to influence the field. Furthermore, being model-agnostic, MagicComp can be used with variety of T2V models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Training-free nature: Reduces computational cost and inference overhead.
    *   Model-agnostic design: Adaptable to various T2V architectures.
    *   Demonstrated improvements: Quantitatively and qualitatively outperforms state-of-the-art methods on standard benchmarks.
    *   Well-defined modules: SAD and DLFA have clear purpose and implementation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Reliance on LLM prior: The DLFA module depends on the quality of the LLM-generated layout priors. The performance could be limited if the LLM prior contains errors.
    *   Limited Scope: The authors acknowledge limitations, and performance relies the pre-trained backbone T2V network.
    *   Qualitative Results: While generally positive, certain edge cases where the approach might fail are not thoroughly explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** The framework's efficiency, performance gains, and training-free nature make it attractive to researchers and practitioners in the field of T2V generation. It could become a building block for more advanced compositional video generation systems.

**Rationale:** The dual-phase approach and design choice, combined with the quantitative results showing improvement over SOTA warrants a relatively high score, particularly the training-free characteristic which could influence the field. However, the novelty is good but not exceptional because it relies upon and adapts existing principles and techniques and is not tackling the more difficult task of training an entire model. Certain limitations are also acknowledged.

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PLAY2PROMPT: Zero-shot Tool Instruction Optimization for LLM Agents via Tool Play
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14432v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Fang, Yang Zhang, Kaizhi Qian, James Glass, Yada Zhu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly integrated with specialized external tools, yet many tasks demand zero-shot tool usage with minimal or noisy documentation. Existing solutions rely on manual rewriting or labeled data for validation, making them inapplicable in true zero-shot settings. To address these challenges, we propose PLAY2PROMPT, an automated framework that systematically "plays" with each tool to explore its input-output behaviors. Through this iterative trial-and-error process, PLAY2PROMPT refines tool documentation and generates usage examples without any labeled data. These examples not only guide LLM inference but also serve as validation to further enhance tool utilization. Extensive experiments on real-world tasks demonstrate that PLAY2PROMPT significantly improves zero-shot tool performance across both open and closed models, offering a scalable and effective solution for domain-specific tool integration.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the PLAY2PROMPT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PLAY2PROMPT, a novel automated framework designed to improve zero-shot tool utilization for LLM agents.  It addresses the problem of LLMs struggling to use new, user-defined tools effectively due to incomplete or missing documentation and lack of example usages.  PLAY2PROMPT operates by iteratively "playing" with each tool through a trial-and-error process, observing successful and failed attempts. This process refines tool documentation and generates usage examples without relying on labeled data or human intervention. The framework utilizes beam search with self-reflection to guide the tool exploration and refinement process. Experiments on real-world tasks, including the Berkeley Function-Calling Leaderboard and StableToolBench, demonstrate that PLAY2PROMPT significantly enhances zero-shot tool performance for both open and closed-source LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of automated tool interaction and "playing" with tools to learn their behavior and then using this knowledge to refine documentation and generate usage examples is innovative. It addresses a key limitation of current prompting-based tool utilization methods. The use of self-reflection within a beam search framework to guide this process is also a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:** This work directly tackles a critical problem in scaling LLM agents: the difficulty of integrating new, domain-specific tools without significant manual effort. By automating the process of tool understanding and documentation creation, PLAY2PROMPT has the potential to reduce the burden on users and developers, making LLM agents more adaptable and useful in diverse real-world scenarios. The fact that this is fully zero-shot is extremely valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Automated and Scalable:** The framework is fully automated and doesn't require any human labeled data, making it highly scalable to new tools and domains.
    *   **Task-Agnostic:** The approach is inherently task-agnostic, further increasing its applicability.
    *   **Empirically Validated:** The extensive experiments on established benchmarks provide strong evidence of the effectiveness of PLAY2PROMPT, consistently outperforming baselines across different LLMs.
    *   **Addresses a Core Problem:** The paper focuses on a fundamental challenge hindering the widespread adoption of LLM agents in real-world applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Single Tool Usage:**  The current implementation focuses primarily on generating examples where only a single tool is used.  While the results suggest that this is sufficient to learn how to solve queries requiring multiple tools, it's a potential limitation. How it extends to truly complex multi-tool scenarios might need further investigation.
    *   **Limited to Simple Tool Invocation:** The reliance on rejection sampling for tool invocation parameters could be a bottleneck for tools with complex parameter spaces (e.g., requiring authentication or long ID strings).  The paper mentions this limitation, suggesting future work may be needed to improve tool play in such cases. The focus on single turn prompting with a set of tools is limiting. Many agents work with a history of tools calls.
    *   **Dependency on LLMs:** Like most LLM-based approaches, PLAY2PROMPT's performance is tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLMs used for generation and evaluation. Although the experiments use multiple LLMs, its effectiveness with less capable models might be lower.

*   **Potential Influence:** PLAY2PROMPT has the potential to significantly influence how LLM agents are integrated with new tools, paving the way for more adaptable and user-friendly systems. The idea of automated tool exploration and refinement could be adopted and extended by other researchers and developers.

**Rigorous Rationale:**
PLAY2PROMPT addresses an essential problem in the LLM agent space with a novel and well-executed approach. The results are compelling, demonstrating significant performance improvements over existing methods across different benchmarks and LLMs. The automation and task-agnostic nature of the framework are particularly valuable. While there are some limitations related to complex tool invocation and reliance on LLM capabilities, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The paper offers a significant contribution to the field and has the potential to drive further research and development in tool utilization for LLM agents.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLM-FE: Automated Feature Engineering for Tabular Data with LLMs as Evolutionary Optimizers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14434v1)
- **Authors**: Nikhil Abhyankar, Parshin Shojaee, Chandan K. Reddy
- **Abstract**: Automated feature engineering plays a critical role in improving predictive model performance for tabular learning tasks. Traditional automated feature engineering methods are limited by their reliance on pre-defined transformations within fixed, manually designed search spaces, often neglecting domain knowledge. Recent advances using Large Language Models (LLMs) have enabled the integration of domain knowledge into the feature engineering process. However, existing LLM-based approaches use direct prompting or rely solely on validation scores for feature selection, failing to leverage insights from prior feature discovery experiments or establish meaningful reasoning between feature generation and data-driven performance. To address these challenges, we propose LLM-FE, a novel framework that combines evolutionary search with the domain knowledge and reasoning capabilities of LLMs to automatically discover effective features for tabular learning tasks. LLM-FE formulates feature engineering as a program search problem, where LLMs propose new feature transformation programs iteratively, and data-driven feedback guides the search process. Our results demonstrate that LLM-FE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, significantly enhancing the performance of tabular prediction models across diverse classification and regression benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LLM-FE: Automated Feature Engineering for Tabular Data with LLMs as Evolutionary Optimizers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-FE, a novel framework for automated feature engineering in tabular data. It combines the domain knowledge and reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) with evolutionary search techniques.  LLM-FE works by iteratively generating feature transformation hypotheses using an LLM, evaluating these hypotheses with data-driven feedback, and refining them through an evolutionary process. The performance of a tabular prediction model trained on data augmented with the generated features guides the LLM's feature creation.  The authors demonstrate that LLM-FE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines across a variety of classification and regression benchmark datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of combining LLMs with evolutionary algorithms for feature engineering is a significant step forward compared to existing LLM-based approaches, which rely heavily on direct prompting or simple validation scores. The paper addresses a key limitation of current LLM-based feature engineering by explicitly incorporating a mechanism to learn from past feature discovery experiments. The multi-population memory and iterative refinement process are well-motivated and contribute to the novelty.

* **Significance:** The paper makes a compelling case for the significance of its approach. Feature engineering is a crucial and often challenging aspect of tabular data analysis. The demonstration that LLM-FE can consistently improve the performance of tabular prediction models, even outperforming models augmented by features derived from other feature engineering methods, suggests a potentially significant impact. This is especially relevant given the increasing interest in tabular data analysis and the recent success of LLMs in various domains.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough experimental evaluation across a diverse range of datasets, predictive models (XGBoost, MLP, TabPFN), and LLM backbones (GPT-3.5-Turbo, Llama-3.1-8B).  The inclusion of large-scale and high-dimensional datasets strengthens the evaluation.
    * **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are well-designed and provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of LLM-FE (domain knowledge, evolutionary refinement, data examples). They convincingly demonstrate the importance of combining these elements.
    * **Qualitative Analysis:** The qualitative analysis provides examples of how LLM-FE leverages domain knowledge to generate more interpretable and relevant features than a domain-agnostic approach. This helps to explain the performance gains.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the LLM-FE framework and its components. The figures and tables are helpful in understanding the approach and results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:** While the paper acknowledges a maximum execution time threshold, it lacks a detailed discussion of the computational cost associated with LLM-FE. The cost of querying LLMs and training prediction models iteratively could be a barrier to adoption, especially for larger datasets or more complex tasks. A more thorough analysis of computational efficiency would be beneficial.
    * **Prompt Engineering Sensitivity:** The performance of LLM-FE likely depends on the quality of the initial prompt and the chosen hyperparameters for the evolutionary search. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of prompt engineering strategies and hyperparameter tuning guidelines to help users effectively apply LLM-FE.
    * **Scope of Feature Types:** Although demonstrated to work across a number of tabular datasets, there's a relative lack of discussion on limitations in generated feature complexity. Will the proposed evolutionary optimization be able to generate very complex features beyond simple combinations? Are there specific scenarios where LLM-FE might struggle compared to hand-crafted features by a human expert?
    * **Code Availability and Reproducibility:** While the authors state that they plan to release their code, its absence at the time of publication is a minor drawback.  Open-sourcing the code is crucial for the wider adoption and further development of LLM-FE.

* **Potential Influence:**  LLM-FE has the potential to significantly influence the field of automated feature engineering for tabular data. It provides a practical and effective approach to leverage LLMs for feature discovery. The framework could be extended to other data-centric tasks, such as data cleaning and data augmentation. The ideas presented in the paper could also inspire new research on combining LLMs with other optimization techniques for various machine learning tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

LLM-FE is a well-executed and novel framework that demonstrates a clear advantage over existing methods for automated feature engineering. The comprehensive evaluation, insightful ablation studies, and qualitative analysis provide strong support for the claims made in the paper. The weaknesses, while important, do not detract significantly from the overall contribution. It is therefore, a worthwhile addition to the tabular learning literature.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### EnvBench: A Benchmark for Automated Environment Setup
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14443v1)
- **Authors**: Aleksandra Eliseeva, Alexander Kovrigin, Ilia Kholkin, Egor Bogomolov, Yaroslav Zharov
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) have enabled researchers to focus on practical repository-level tasks in software engineering domain. In this work, we consider a cornerstone task for automating work with software repositories-environment setup, i.e., a task of configuring a repository-specific development environment on a system. Existing studies on environment setup introduce innovative agentic strategies, but their evaluation is often based on small datasets that may not capture the full range of configuration challenges encountered in practice. To address this gap, we introduce a comprehensive environment setup benchmark EnvBench. It encompasses 329 Python and 665 JVM-based (Java, Kotlin) repositories, with a focus on repositories that present genuine configuration challenges, excluding projects that can be fully configured by simple deterministic scripts. To enable further benchmark extension and usage for model tuning, we implement two automatic metrics: a static analysis check for missing imports in Python and a compilation check for JVM languages. We demonstrate the applicability of our benchmark by evaluating three environment setup approaches, including a simple zero-shot baseline and two agentic workflows, that we test with two powerful LLM backbones, GPT-4o and GPT-4o-mini. The best approach manages to successfully configure 6.69% repositories for Python and 29.47% repositories for JVM, suggesting that EnvBench remains challenging for current approaches. Our benchmark suite is publicly available at https://github.com/JetBrains-Research/EnvBench. The dataset and experiment trajectories are available at https://jb.gg/envbench.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces ENVBENCH, a new benchmark for automated environment setup of software repositories, addressing a gap in existing research that primarily focuses on agentic strategies with limited benchmarking. ENVBENCH encompasses 329 Python and 665 JVM-based repositories, carefully filtered to present genuine configuration challenges not solvable by simple scripts. The paper includes automatic metrics (static analysis for Python and compilation checks for JVM languages) for evaluating setup success. The benchmark is applied to evaluate three environment setup approaches (zero-shot LLM, Bash Agent, Installamatic Agent) with two LLM backbones (GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini). The results highlight the difficulty of the task, with the best approach achieving only 6.69% success on Python and 29.47% on JVM repositories, while also uncovering issues such as the generation of erroneous scripts and performance disparities between different agents and backbones.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Real-World Problem:** Automated environment setup is a practical and vital task in software engineering, directly impacting developer productivity and reproducibility.
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** ENVBENCH is a significant contribution due to its scale (number of repositories) and diversity (Python and JVM ecosystems), filling a void in the current literature where benchmarks are smaller and less representative.
    *   **Rigorous Filtering:** The authors' careful filtering process, excluding trivial cases and Docker-reliant projects, ensures the benchmark targets genuine configuration challenges.
    *   **Automated Evaluation:** The introduction of automated evaluation metrics is valuable for scaling evaluations and enabling further model tuning, although their limitations are acknowledged.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough empirical evaluation of several environment setup approaches with different LLM backbones.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Success Rates:** The relatively low success rates of even the best-performing approach (29.47% for JVM and 6.69% for Python) suggest that while ENVBENCH is challenging, it might currently expose limitations in current LLMs and agent architectures, rather than providing insights for practical real-world applications. The low rates indicate a need for substantial improvements in the field before automated environment setup becomes reliable.
    *   **Metric Limitations:** Static analysis and compilation checks, while scalable, are proxies for actual environment correctness and might miss runtime issues that could drastically affect a repository's functionality. The authors acknowledge this limitation. A test-suite based approach can mitigate this, albeit at the cost of manual effort for benchmark creation.
    *   **Benchmark evolution:** Because environment tooling changes rapidly, the currency of the benchmark has to be guaranteed by continuously adding new repositories.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper offers genuine novelty. Prior work focuses more on agentic strategies than on providing robust benchmarks. The creation of a large, curated dataset targeting complex environment setup scenarios for Python and JVM and two automatic verification mechanisms clearly advances the field. Its significance stems from providing a standardized platform for researchers to evaluate and compare different automated environment setup approaches in a rigorous and scalable manner. It also highlights the challenges faced by current LLM-based approaches and provides a valuable starting point for future research directions. However, the limitation regarding the metric's lack of true runtime validation (and the impact on real-world applicability) hinders overall significance.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    ENVBENCH has the potential to influence the field significantly by:

    *   Driving the development of more sophisticated and robust automated environment setup techniques.
    *   Facilitating the comparison of different approaches on a standardized and challenging benchmark.
    *   Encouraging the development of more accurate and reliable evaluation metrics for environment setup.
    *   Ultimately, improving developer productivity and reproducibility by automating a crucial task.

Score: 6.5

**Rationale:**

While ENVBENCH is a valuable contribution to the field, filling a crucial gap in benchmarks for automated environment setup, its current limitations (particularly the low success rates achieved by the best methods and reliance on proxy metrics) temper its overall impact. The benchmark is well-constructed, the evaluation is thorough, and the paper clearly articulates its contributions and limitations. But the relatively low success achieved on the benchmark indicates it may be ahead of current technological capabilities. If this limitation can be addressed by for example including a broader range of evaluation metrics, the significance would be elevated, making the task more challenging. A more robust runtime-level verification could boost the score to 8.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Bolt3D: Generating 3D Scenes in Seconds
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14445v1)
- **Authors**: Stanislaw Szymanowicz, Jason Y. Zhang, Pratul Srinivasan, Ruiqi Gao, Arthur Brussee, Aleksander Holynski, Ricardo Martin-Brualla, Jonathan T. Barron, Philipp Henzler
- **Abstract**: We present a latent diffusion model for fast feed-forward 3D scene generation. Given one or more images, our model Bolt3D directly samples a 3D scene representation in less than seven seconds on a single GPU. We achieve this by leveraging powerful and scalable existing 2D diffusion network architectures to produce consistent high-fidelity 3D scene representations. To train this model, we create a large-scale multiview-consistent dataset of 3D geometry and appearance by applying state-of-the-art dense 3D reconstruction techniques to existing multiview image datasets. Compared to prior multiview generative models that require per-scene optimization for 3D reconstruction, Bolt3D reduces the inference cost by a factor of up to 300 times.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Bolt3D paper:

**Summary:**

The Bolt3D paper introduces a novel latent diffusion model for generating 3D scenes in a feed-forward manner, directly from one or more input images. The model leverages the advancements in scalable 2D diffusion architectures to produce consistent, high-fidelity 3D scene representations in seconds. It represents 3D scenes using 3D Gaussians, stored in multiple 2D grids ("Splatter Images"). Key components include a geometry VAE trained on a large-scale multiview-consistent dataset created using Structure-from-Motion on existing image datasets, and a Gaussian head network that predicts opacities, shapes, and refines colors of the 3D Gaussians.  Bolt3D outperforms existing single and few-view feed-forward 3D regression methods and achieves significant speed improvements compared to multiview image generation methods that rely on per-scene optimization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Speed and Efficiency:** The most significant strength is the feed-forward architecture, enabling very fast generation (seconds) compared to methods that require per-scene optimization (minutes/hours).  The paper highlights a reduction of inference cost by up to 300x, which is a substantial improvement.
    *   **Generative Approach for Ambiguity:** The paper correctly identifies and addresses the limitations of purely regression-based methods in handling ambiguity in few-view reconstruction.  The generative nature of Bolt3D allows it to hallucinate plausible content in unobserved regions.
    *   **Large-Scale Training Data:** Creating a large-scale multiview-consistent dataset is crucial for training a generalizable 3D generative model, and the paper makes a valuable contribution by outlining how this can be achieved using existing datasets and Structure-from-Motion.
    *   **Effective 3D Representation:** Leveraging 3D Gaussians and "Splatter Images" allows for efficient rendering and a balance between detail and computational cost.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Structure-from-Motion:** While the use of Structure-from-Motion is beneficial for creating the training data, it inherently limits the method to scenes where SfM works well. Scenes with significant transparency, reflections, or textureless surfaces may pose challenges for SfM, and therefore, for the quality of Bolt3D's training data. The paper acknowledges some of these limitations.
    *   **Qualitative Assessment:** The majority of the evaluation relies on qualitative results and standard metrics for novel view synthesis. While these are informative, a more thorough analysis including user studies or task-specific evaluations, would further strengthen the paper.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is effective, many of the individual components (latent diffusion, VAEs, 3D Gaussians) are well-established. The primary novelty lies in the *specific architecture* and *training strategy* that integrates these components for fast, feed-forward 3D scene generation.
    *   **Limited Ablations:** The paper mentions referring the reader to the supplementary material. While this isn't inherently bad, critical and crucial ablation studies should be mentioned within the main body of the paper. This would make the paper stronger, rather than pushing key elements to the supplement.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's novelty lies primarily in the successful integration of a latent diffusion model, geometry VAE, and Gaussian splatting representation for fast, feed-forward 3D scene generation from limited input views.  This approach addresses a critical bottleneck in 3D scene reconstruction and generation by significantly improving speed without sacrificing visual quality.
    *   The significance stems from the potential to enable a wider range of applications that require real-time 3D scene generation, such as interactive visualization, virtual reality, and content creation.  The ability to generate 3D scenes from a few images with minimal processing time is a substantial step forward.
    *   The creation of a large-scale training dataset is also a significant contribution that can benefit future research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

Bolt3D represents a solid advancement in the field of 3D scene generation, demonstrating a practical approach to accelerate the process significantly. However, the incremental nature of the novelty, dependence on SfM and potential quality limitations, along with the reliance on other aspects present in the supplement lead to a slightly lower rating. It still makes a significant impact, specifically regarding speed and by introducing a novel architecture.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### RWKV-7 "Goose" with Expressive Dynamic State Evolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14456v1)
- **Authors**: Bo Peng, Ruichong Zhang, Daniel Goldstein, Eric Alcaide, Haowen Hou, Janna Lu, William Merrill, Guangyu Song, Kaifeng Tan, Saiteja Utpala, Nathan Wilce, Johan S. Wind, Tianyi Wu, Daniel Wuttke, Christian Zhou-Zheng
- **Abstract**: We present RWKV-7 "Goose", a new sequence modeling architecture, along with pre-trained language models that establish a new state-of-the-art in downstream performance at the 3 billion parameter scale on multilingual tasks, and match current SoTA English language performance despite being trained on dramatically fewer tokens than other top 3B models. Nevertheless, RWKV-7 models require only constant memory usage and constant inference time per token. RWKV-7 introduces a newly generalized formulation of the delta rule with vector-valued gating and in-context learning rates, as well as a relaxed value replacement rule. We show that RWKV-7 can perform state tracking and recognize all regular languages, while retaining parallelizability of training. This exceeds the capabilities of Transformers under standard complexity conjectures, which are limited to $\mathsf{TC}^0$. To demonstrate RWKV-7's language modeling capability, we also present an extended open source 3.1 trillion token multilingual corpus, and train four RWKV-7 models ranging from 0.19 billion to 2.9 billion parameters on this dataset. To foster openness, reproduction, and adoption, we release our models and dataset component listing at https://huggingface.co/RWKV, and our training and inference code at https://github.com/RWKV/RWKV-LM all under the Apache 2.0 License.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the RWKV-7 "Goose" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RWKV-7 "Goose," a new sequence modeling architecture that builds upon the recurrent neural network (RNN) foundation of previous RWKV models. It proposes a generalized delta rule with enhancements like vector-valued state gating, in-context learning rates, and relaxed value replacement. The authors theoretically prove RWKV-7's improved expressivity, demonstrating it can recognize all regular languages, exceeding the limitations of Transformers under common complexity assumptions.  They train a series of RWKV-7 models (up to 2.9B parameters) on a new 3.1 trillion token multilingual dataset ("RWKV World v3") and demonstrate strong performance on downstream tasks, rivaling Transformer-based models of similar size, especially on multilingual benchmarks, while maintaining constant memory usage and inference time per token. They also present multimodal experiments, demonstrating the model's ability to incorporate visual information. The models, dataset components, and code are publicly released under permissive licenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the generalized delta rule, which incorporates vector-valued gating and learning rates into the state update mechanism.  The theoretical result concerning the ability to recognize all regular languages is also a significant contribution. While incremental improvements to the RWKV architecture, the introduction of the RWKV World v3 dataset and open-source releases are valuable additions to the field.
*   **Significance:** The paper makes a strong case for the continued viability of RNN-like architectures in sequence modeling. The demonstrated performance, especially in multilingual scenarios, at a lower inference cost than Transformers, is significant. The theoretical expressivity results challenge conventional wisdom regarding the limitations of RNNs. The open-source release of models, code, and dataset promotes reproducibility and adoption.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Theoretical Foundation:**  The paper provides rigorous mathematical proofs for the improved expressivity of RWKV-7.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  Extensive experiments demonstrate the practical benefits of the proposed architecture.
    *   **Multilingual Performance:** RWKV-7 exhibits excellent performance on multilingual benchmarks, where Transformers can be less efficient.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:**  The constant memory and inference time are crucial for long sequences.
    *   **Open Source Release:**  Promotes research and application.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** The architectural changes, while significant, are evolutionary rather than revolutionary.  RWKV-7 builds heavily upon prior RWKV models.
    *   **Limited Scale:** While the 2.9B parameter models are competitive, they are small relative to the largest current language models.  Scaling RWKV-7 to larger sizes will be essential to demonstrate its full potential.
    *   **Training from Checkpoints:** Some models were trained from existing checkpoints rather than from scratch, which may limit their potential.
    *   **Performance on Long Contexts (in some configurations):** The paper notes issues with long contexts that require special handling and fine-tuning.
    *   **Instruction tuning and Alignment: The architecture has not been instruction tuned or aligned. This could reduce performance on some general language models and prevent use as a chat assistant without further tuning
    *   **Prompt Sensitivity: The architecture is shown to be sensitive to prompt construction (absence of the special token < | endoftext | >)
*   **Impact on the Field:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research into RNN-like architectures for sequence modeling. It encourages exploration of expressivity beyond the standard Transformer framework.  The open-source resources will facilitate experimentation and application.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**. The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by advancing RNN architectures and demonstrating their competitiveness with Transformers in certain scenarios. The theoretical results and the commitment to open science are particularly commendable. The incremental nature of the improvements, especially when compared to the most recent advancements of instruction tuning and alignment, the limited scale of the models and the challenges when considering particularly long lengths, prevent the score from being higher, but is still a highly valuable contribution to sequence modeling.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### SIR-DIFF: Sparse Image Sets Restoration with Multi-View Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14463v1)
- **Authors**: Yucheng Mao, Boyang Wang, Nilesh Kulkarni, Jeong Joon Park
- **Abstract**: The computer vision community has developed numerous techniques for digitally restoring true scene information from single-view degraded photographs, an important yet extremely ill-posed task. In this work, we tackle image restoration from a different perspective by jointly denoising multiple photographs of the same scene. Our core hypothesis is that degraded images capturing a shared scene contain complementary information that, when combined, better constrains the restoration problem. To this end, we implement a powerful multi-view diffusion model that jointly generates uncorrupted views by extracting rich information from multi-view relationships. Our experiments show that our multi-view approach outperforms existing single-view image and even video-based methods on image deblurring and super-resolution tasks. Critically, our model is trained to output 3D consistent images, making it a promising tool for applications requiring robust multi-view integration, such as 3D reconstruction or pose estimation.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper titled "SIR-DIFF: Sparse Image Sets Restoration with Multi-View Diffusion Model" presents a novel approach to image restoration by utilizing multiple degraded photographs of the same scene. It posits that such images contain complementary information, offering a richer source for restoration compared to single-view methods. To implement this idea, the authors develop a multi-view diffusion model that generates uncorrupted images by leveraging relationships among multiple views. Their experiments demonstrate that this multi-view strategy surpasses traditional single-view and even video methods in tasks like image deblurring and super-resolution. Importantly, the model ensures 3D consistency in the generated images, which positions it well for applications in 3D reconstruction and pose estimation. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of this paper lies in its shift from conventional single-image restoration approaches to a multi-view framework. This is a significant direction, as it acknowledges the potential for richer data redundancy and complementary information in images of the same scene. The application of a diffusion model is particularly timely, given the recent advancements in generative models, and it showcases innovative use of such technologies in practical restoration tasks. Strengths: 1. **Innovative Approach**: The integration of multi-view perspectives in the restoration process is relatively unexplored, making this a significant advance over typical single-view methodologies. 2. **Performance**: The results indicate substantial improvements in restoration tasks, suggesting that the method is not only theoretical but also practically effective. 3. **3D Consistency**: Training the model for 3D consistency could lead to robust applications in fields like augmented reality and 3D reconstruction.
