Weaknesses: 1. **Limited Scope**: The experiments may need more diverse datasets to validate the model's efficacy across a wider range of scenarios. 2. **Complexity**: The proposed model's complexity could hamper real-time applications, which are critical for certain uses in the industry. 3. **Dependence on Multi-view Data**: The model's reliance on having multiple views may limit its applicability in situations where only single views are available. Overall, while the approach is promising and demonstrates clear advances in restoring degraded images, the potential limitations in its applicability and complexity warrant a balanced assessment. Given these considerations, I would rate the paper's novelty and significance as follows: **Score: 8** This score reflects the paper's robust contribution to the field and its innovative methodology while recognizing the existing challenges that could affect its broader impact.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Creation-MMBench: Assessing Context-Aware Creative Intelligence in MLLM
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14478v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyu Fang, Zhijian Chen, Kai Lan, Shengyuan Ding, Yingji Liang, Xiangyu Zhao, Farong Wen, Zicheng Zhang, Guofeng Zhang, Haodong Duan, Kai Chen, Dahua Lin
- **Abstract**: Creativity is a fundamental aspect of intelligence, involving the ability to generate novel and appropriate solutions across diverse contexts. While Large Language Models (LLMs) have been extensively evaluated for their creative capabilities, the assessment of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in this domain remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we introduce Creation-MMBench, a multimodal benchmark specifically designed to evaluate the creative capabilities of MLLMs in real-world, image-based tasks. The benchmark comprises 765 test cases spanning 51 fine-grained tasks. To ensure rigorous evaluation, we define instance-specific evaluation criteria for each test case, guiding the assessment of both general response quality and factual consistency with visual inputs. Experimental results reveal that current open-source MLLMs significantly underperform compared to proprietary models in creative tasks. Furthermore, our analysis demonstrates that visual fine-tuning can negatively impact the base LLM's creative abilities. Creation-MMBench provides valuable insights for advancing MLLM creativity and establishes a foundation for future improvements in multimodal generative intelligence. Full data and evaluation code is released on https://github.com/open-compass/Creation-MMBench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Creation-MMBench: Assessing Context-Aware Creative Intelligence in MLLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces *Creation-MMBench*, a new multimodal benchmark designed to evaluate the creative capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in real-world, image-based tasks. It addresses a gap in current MLLM evaluations, which tend to focus on analytical and contextual intelligence while neglecting creative intelligence. The benchmark consists of 765 test cases spanning 51 fine-grained tasks across four categories: Literary Writing, Common Functional Writing, Professional Functional Writing, and Creative Multimodal Understanding.  A novel evaluation methodology is implemented, including instance-specific criteria assessed by GPT-4o, utilizing both pairwise comparison and unitary scoring to ensure the integration of contextual and visual information. The paper also examines the impact of visual fine-tuning on creative abilities by comparing MLLM performance to a text-only variant of the benchmark, revealing potentially negative effects of such tuning.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Clear Gap:** The paper effectively identifies a crucial area lacking in MLLM evaluation: creative intelligence within real-world, image-based scenarios. Current benchmarks tend to emphasize analytical capabilities, making Creation-MMBench a valuable and timely contribution.

*   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The size (765 test cases), variety (51 fine-grained tasks across different categories), and diversity of image sources make Creation-MMBench a robust and thorough evaluation tool. The categories are well-defined and reflect the multi-faceted nature of creative intelligence.

*   **Novel Evaluation Methodology:** The instance-specific evaluation criteria and the use of GPT-4o as a judge, coupled with pairwise comparison and visual factuality scoring, provide a more nuanced and rigorous assessment than existing methods. Using a large language model for evaluation is justified by the complex and often subjective nature of creative tasks, for which it's difficult to define strict, rule-based metrics.

*   **Important Findings:** The experimental results revealing the underperformance of open-source MLLMs compared to proprietary models, and the potential negative impact of visual fine-tuning on creative abilities are significant findings that prompt further research into the complexities of MLLM development.  The construction of Creation-MMBench-TO as a text-only variant to further study the impact of visual instruction is well-reasoned.

*   **Open and Reproducible:** The release of data and evaluation code enhances the transparency and reproducibility of the research, enabling further studies and extensions of the benchmark.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on GPT-4o:** The reliance on GPT-4o for evaluation introduces a potential bias, as the benchmark effectively compares the evaluated MLLMs against GPT-4o's understanding of creative intelligence. While the justification for using an LLM judge is strong, acknowledging and mitigating this bias could improve the evaluation's robustness.

*   **Subjectivity in Ground Truth:**  While the paper describes a rigorous quality control process for the dataset, the design of creative tasks and the definition of "correct" responses inherently involve subjectivity. The authors attempt to mitigate this through instance-specific criteria, but acknowledge that human preferences can still vary considerably.

*   **Limited Generalizability of Negative Fine-Tuning Impact:** While the negative impact of visual fine-tuning on creative abilities is an interesting observation, the study's scope is limited to the specific models and tasks included in the benchmark. Further research is needed to determine the generalizability of this finding across a wider range of MLLMs and creative domains.

*   **Difficulty in isolating Visual Contribution:**  Even with Creation-MMBench-TO, it is difficult to completely isolate the value of visual input, since LLMs may have "seen" the same or similar images during their pre-training phase. As such, pre-existing visual knowledge will contribute even in Creation-MMBench-TO, making comparisons more difficult.

**Significance and Potential Influence:**

Creation-MMBench is a significant contribution to the field of MLLMs, offering a comprehensive and rigorous benchmark for evaluating a critical but often overlooked aspect of intelligence: creative abilities. Its focus on real-world tasks, coupled with a novel evaluation methodology, provides valuable insights into the current limitations of MLLMs and guides future research directions. The findings regarding visual fine-tuning have particularly important implications for MLLM development strategies. The benchmark has the potential to become a standard evaluation tool in the field, driving progress in multimodal generative intelligence.

**Score:** 8.5/10

**Justification:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution to the field by addressing a significant gap in MLLM evaluation with a well-designed and comprehensive benchmark. While the reliance on GPT-4o for evaluation and the inherent subjectivity of creative tasks introduce potential limitations, the rigor of the methodology, the importance of the findings, and the open accessibility of the benchmark materials justify a high score. The paper's potential influence on future MLLM research and development makes it a valuable addition to the literature.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ICE-Bench: A Unified and Comprehensive Benchmark for Image Creating and Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14482v1)
- **Authors**: Yulin Pan, Xiangteng He, Chaojie Mao, Zhen Han, Zeyinzi Jiang, Jingfeng Zhang, Yu Liu
- **Abstract**: Image generation has witnessed significant advancements in the past few years. However, evaluating the performance of image generation models remains a formidable challenge. In this paper, we propose ICE-Bench, a unified and comprehensive benchmark designed to rigorously assess image generation models. Its comprehensiveness could be summarized in the following key features: (1) Coarse-to-Fine Tasks: We systematically deconstruct image generation into four task categories: No-ref/Ref Image Creating/Editing, based on the presence or absence of source images and reference images. And further decompose them into 31 fine-grained tasks covering a broad spectrum of image generation requirements, culminating in a comprehensive benchmark. (2) Multi-dimensional Metrics: The evaluation framework assesses image generation capabilities across 6 dimensions: aesthetic quality, imaging quality, prompt following, source consistency, reference consistency, and controllability. 11 metrics are introduced to support the multi-dimensional evaluation. Notably, we introduce VLLM-QA, an innovative metric designed to assess the success of image editing by leveraging large models. (3) Hybrid Data: The data comes from real scenes and virtual generation, which effectively improves data diversity and alleviates the bias problem in model evaluation. Through ICE-Bench, we conduct a thorough analysis of existing generation models, revealing both the challenging nature of our benchmark and the gap between current model capabilities and real-world generation requirements. To foster further advancements in the field, we will open-source ICE-Bench, including its dataset, evaluation code, and models, thereby providing a valuable resource for the research community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ICE-Bench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ICE-Bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate image generation and editing models in a unified and comprehensive manner. The benchmark covers 31 fine-grained tasks, categorized into coarse-to-fine levels based on the type of generation (creating vs. editing) and the use of reference images.  ICE-Bench uses a multi-dimensional evaluation framework assessing aesthetic quality, imaging quality, prompt following, source consistency, reference consistency, and controllability, utilizing 11 metrics. The dataset includes both real-world and virtually generated images to improve diversity and reduce bias. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art models using ICE-Bench, highlighting their strengths and weaknesses. The benchmark, dataset, evaluation code, and models will be publicly released to foster further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's main novelty lies in the combination of several aspects: a unified and extensive benchmark encompassing diverse image generation and editing tasks, a multi-dimensional evaluation framework, and a hybrid dataset that aims to mitigate bias. While individual components (e.g., evaluating with multiple metrics, using both real and synthetic data) have been explored in previous works, the integrated approach of ICE-Bench represents a significant step forward. The hierarchical organization of tasks (coarse-to-fine) is a thoughtful design choice, allowing for granular analysis.  The use of VLLM-QA as a metric for assessing instruction execution is innovative and addresses a key limitation of relying solely on CLIP similarity for editing tasks.
*   **Significance:**  The significance of ICE-Bench is that it provides a standardized platform for evaluating and comparing the performance of image generation models across a wide range of capabilities. This is crucial because the field currently lacks a comprehensive benchmark, leading to inconsistent and difficult-to-compare results. ICE-Bench can help researchers identify areas where models need improvement and guide the development of more robust and versatile image generation techniques. The public release of the benchmark will further enhance its impact by enabling wider adoption and contribution from the research community. The benchmark is specifically designed to address the limitations of current datasets by covering diverse evaluation dimension and focusing on evaluating various types of image generation tasks from no-ref creating to reference image editing.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Task Coverage:** The benchmark includes a wide variety of image generation and editing tasks, making it more representative of real-world applications.
    *   **Multi-Dimensional Evaluation:** The evaluation framework goes beyond simple metrics like FID and IS, providing a more nuanced assessment of model performance.
    *   **Hybrid Data:** The use of both real and synthetic data helps to reduce bias and improve the generalizability of the evaluation.
    *   **VLLM-QA metric:** the use of VLLMs to infer successful image edition is innovative and addresses a major limitation of existing image editing benchmarks
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The sheer number of tasks and metrics could make it challenging for researchers to fully analyze and interpret the results.  A simpler, more focused benchmark might be easier to adopt initially.
    *   **Metric limitations:** While the authors introduce VLLM-QA, the core metrics still rely on image quality measures that might be blind to some specific failure modes and tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:** ICE-Bench has the potential to become a widely adopted benchmark in the image generation and editing field. It could help to drive progress by providing a clear and objective way to compare different models and identify areas for improvement.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** ICE-Bench is a significant and valuable contribution to the image generation field. While it has some weaknesses related to complexity and dependence on VLLM, its strengths – comprehensive task coverage, multi-dimensional evaluation, innovative metric, and open access – outweigh these limitations. The benchmark is a crucial resource that will likely have a substantial impact on the development and evaluation of future image generation models. ICE-Bench, through its open access nature and comprehensive construction, may serve as an anchor for the community, offering both new insights and challenges to existing image generation models. The adoption of the new benchmark by the broader research community would greatly benefit the field by establishing a common evaluation standard.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Lux Post Facto: Learning Portrait Performance Relighting with Conditional Video Diffusion and a Hybrid Dataset
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14485v1)
- **Authors**: Yiqun Mei, Mingming He, Li Ma, Julien Philip, Wenqi Xian, David M George, Xueming Yu, Gabriel Dedic, Ahmet Levent Taşel, Ning Yu, Vishal M. Patel, Paul Debevec
- **Abstract**: Video portrait relighting remains challenging because the results need to be both photorealistic and temporally stable. This typically requires a strong model design that can capture complex facial reflections as well as intensive training on a high-quality paired video dataset, such as dynamic one-light-at-a-time (OLAT). In this work, we introduce Lux Post Facto, a novel portrait video relighting method that produces both photorealistic and temporally consistent lighting effects. From the model side, we design a new conditional video diffusion model built upon state-of-the-art pre-trained video diffusion model, alongside a new lighting injection mechanism to enable precise control. This way we leverage strong spatial and temporal generative capability to generate plausible solutions to the ill-posed relighting problem. Our technique uses a hybrid dataset consisting of static expression OLAT data and in-the-wild portrait performance videos to jointly learn relighting and temporal modeling. This avoids the need to acquire paired video data in different lighting conditions. Our extensive experiments show that our model produces state-of-the-art results both in terms of photorealism and temporal consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Lux Post Facto: Learning Portrait Performance Relighting with Conditional Video Diffusion and a Hybrid Dataset":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Lux Post Facto, a novel method for video portrait relighting that aims to achieve both photorealistic and temporally stable results.  The approach utilizes a conditional video diffusion model fine-tuned on a hybrid dataset. This dataset combines static one-light-at-a-time (OLAT) images with in-the-wild portrait videos.  Key innovations include a new lighting injection mechanism that allows precise control over relighting by encoding HDR maps as a set of lighting embeddings and using cross-attention. The hybrid dataset and training strategy address the challenge of obtaining paired video data in different lighting conditions. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in terms of photorealism and temporal consistency through extensive experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components. The hybrid dataset training approach is a smart way to overcome the data scarcity problem in dynamic relighting. Combining the static OLAT images with in-the-wild videos is a creative solution. The lighting embedding scheme combined with the cross-attention in a video diffusion model also appears to be a novel approach to conditioning. The idea of using "lighting tokens" provides a potentially more robust encoding of HDR environments than simply passing raw pixel values.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in addressing the challenging problem of achieving both photorealism and temporal consistency in video relighting.  Previous methods often struggle to maintain temporal stability or require expensive and difficult-to-acquire data (like dynamic OLAT sequences). By offering a practical and trainable method that delivers high-quality, temporally coherent relighting from standard video, the paper makes an important contribution to the field. The hybrid dataset approach in particular provides a roadmap for other researchers to deal with the difficulty in obtaining paired or fully controlled video data.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.
    *   The hybrid dataset addresses a key challenge in video relighting.
    *   The lighting injection mechanism offers precise control over relighting effects.
    *   The method is practical and relatively easy to train compared to techniques that require specialized capture setups.
    *   Clear and well-written explanation of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a pre-trained diffusion model means that the method's performance is somewhat tied to the capabilities of the underlying model. Improvements in diffusion model architectures might be necessary to scale up to very high resolutions or handle very complex lighting scenarios.
    *   The paper mentions (though does not fully explore) certain failure cases, such as handling very complex occlusions from accessories. A more comprehensive analysis of limitations would further strengthen the paper.
    *   While the method outperforms other approaches, the visual results, while impressive, still occasionally exhibit minor artifacts (particularly in the video results, based on the supplementary material).
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in video relighting, particularly in the development of more data-efficient training strategies and more effective mechanisms for controlling lighting in generative models. The hybrid dataset approach could be adapted to other related problems in video editing and synthesis. The approach could be seen as a milestone towards democratizing video effects that once required sophisticated studio setups.
    *   The method’s adoption is also helped by building on SVD (Stable Video Diffusion).
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The key aspect making this paper stand out from existing efforts on portrait/video relighting is its innovative use of a hybrid training strategy to tackle a hard problem. In that regard it’s more than simply a clever application of a pre-trained diffusion model.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper makes significant, novel contributions to a challenging problem. The hybrid dataset approach, the lighting embedding scheme, and the strong empirical results are all compelling.  While there are limitations (inherent to the use of diffusion models and some challenges with very complex scenes), the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses. Its practical approach with strong performance, makes the paper likely to have a real and lasting influence on the future of video relighting.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 8/10

### DiffMoE: Dynamic Token Selection for Scalable Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14487v1)
- **Authors**: Minglei Shi, Ziyang Yuan, Haotian Yang, Xintao Wang, Mingwu Zheng, Xin Tao, Wenliang Zhao, Wenzhao Zheng, Jie Zhou, Jiwen Lu, Pengfei Wan, Di Zhang, Kun Gai
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have demonstrated remarkable success in various image generation tasks, but their performance is often limited by the uniform processing of inputs across varying conditions and noise levels. To address this limitation, we propose a novel approach that leverages the inherent heterogeneity of the diffusion process. Our method, DiffMoE, introduces a batch-level global token pool that enables experts to access global token distributions during training, promoting specialized expert behavior. To unleash the full potential of the diffusion process, DiffMoE incorporates a capacity predictor that dynamically allocates computational resources based on noise levels and sample complexity. Through comprehensive evaluation, DiffMoE achieves state-of-the-art performance among diffusion models on ImageNet benchmark, substantially outperforming both dense architectures with 3x activated parameters and existing MoE approaches while maintaining 1x activated parameters. The effectiveness of our approach extends beyond class-conditional generation to more challenging tasks such as text-to-image generation, demonstrating its broad applicability across different diffusion model applications. Project Page: https://shiml20.github.io/DiffMoE/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DiffMoE paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffMoE, a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) based architecture for diffusion transformers (DiTs) that addresses the limitations of existing MoE approaches in this domain. DiffMoE uses a batch-level global token pool to enable experts to access a wider range of token distributions, fostering specialized expert behavior.  It also incorporates a capacity predictor to dynamically allocate computational resources based on noise levels and sample complexity. Experimental results on ImageNet show that DiffMoE achieves state-of-the-art performance compared to dense and existing MoE architectures while maintaining efficient scaling properties. The paper also extends DiffMoE to text-to-image generation, showcasing its broad applicability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents two main novelties: 1) the batch-level global token pool, and 2) the capacity predictor for dynamic resource allocation. While the idea of MoE in diffusion models isn't entirely new, the global token pool is a significant architectural change that addresses the token isolation problem faced by existing MoE-DiTs. The dynamic capacity predictor also adds a new dimension, allowing for more efficient computation by adapting to the complexity of different diffusion steps. The authors point to similar ideas around global batches in LLMs but convincingly argue why it is especially crucial for diffusion transformers.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the improved performance and efficiency achieved by DiffMoE. The results demonstrate that DiffMoE surpasses both dense architectures and existing MoE approaches in image generation tasks.  Achieving strong results using comparable resources is highly valuable and demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  The successful application to text-to-image generation further strengthens its significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current MoE approaches in diffusion models, particularly regarding token accessibility and fixed computation.
    *   **Novel Architectural Contributions:** The global token pool and dynamic capacity predictor are well-motivated and designed.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides convincing evidence of DiffMoE's superior performance through extensive experiments on ImageNet and text-to-image generation. Ablation studies validate the importance of each component.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making it easy to follow the proposed approach and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** While the results are compelling, the DiffMoE architecture adds complexity compared to standard dense DiTs.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** MoE models are notoriously difficult to tune. While the paper outlines experimental settings, it provides limited details about how specific MoE-related hyperparameters (e.g., expert capacity, threshold values) were optimized. The reliance on heuristics such as fixed capacity ratios may limit performance.
    *   **Generalization to Other Datasets:** The primary evaluation is on ImageNet. While text-to-image experiments are provided, generalization to other image datasets or different modalities is not explored extensively.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's influence could be significant. The proposed global token pool and dynamic capacity prediction are likely to inspire future research on MoE-based diffusion models. Furthermore, the improved efficiency achieved by DiffMoE could make diffusion models more accessible for resource-constrained environments.

*   **Score Justification:** While the underlying premise of MoE is not novel, the application in the form of a global token pool along with capacity prediction makes this contribution a novel approach. The benefits are also clearly shown. The areas where it could be improved, including analysis of optimal hyperparameters and generalizability to other image datasets slightly detract from the value, but this remains a solid contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Stable Virtual Camera: Generative View Synthesis with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14489v1)
- **Authors**: Jensen, Zhou, Hang Gao, Vikram Voleti, Aaryaman Vasishta, Chun-Han Yao, Mark Boss, Philip Torr, Christian Rupprecht, Varun Jampani
- **Abstract**: We present Stable Virtual Camera (Seva), a generalist diffusion model that creates novel views of a scene, given any number of input views and target cameras. Existing works struggle to generate either large viewpoint changes or temporally smooth samples, while relying on specific task configurations. Our approach overcomes these limitations through simple model design, optimized training recipe, and flexible sampling strategy that generalize across view synthesis tasks at test time. As a result, our samples maintain high consistency without requiring additional 3D representation-based distillation, thus streamlining view synthesis in the wild. Furthermore, we show that our method can generate high-quality videos lasting up to half a minute with seamless loop closure. Extensive benchmarking demonstrates that Seva outperforms existing methods across different datasets and settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the "Stable Virtual Camera" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Stable Virtual Camera (SEVA), a diffusion-based model for novel view synthesis (NVS). SEVA aims to overcome limitations of existing NVS methods by generating high-quality, 3D-consistent novel views across various scenarios, including large viewpoint changes, temporal smoothness, and flexible input/target view configurations. The approach avoids explicit 3D representations within the network, leverages a carefully designed training recipe, and uses a two-pass procedural sampling strategy at inference. The authors demonstrate through extensive benchmarking that SEVA outperforms existing methods on various datasets and settings, even achieving state-of-the-art results in some cases. They also showcase high-quality, long-duration videos with precise camera control and seamless loop closure.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several aspects, rather than a single groundbreaking contribution. It's a holistic engineering effort, carefully balancing architecture, training, and inference strategies.
    *   **Architecture:** The use of a diffusion model conditioned on CLIP embeddings, VAE latents, and camera poses, with modifications to self-attention layers, is an incremental improvement over prior art like CAT3D. The architectural changes by themselves aren't particularly revolutionary.
    *   **Training Strategy:** The two-stage training curriculum and the view selection strategy designed to cover both small and large viewpoint changes contribute significantly to generalization.  This is a key area of innovation.
    *   **Sampling Strategy:** The two-pass procedural sampling, especially the approaches for set NVS and trajectory NVS, is a valuable contribution that allows for flexible input/target configurations.  The memory bank approach for long trajectories is a helpful addition.
* **Significance:**
    *   **Generalization:** The most significant aspect is the generalization capability across different NVS tasks (set NVS, trajectory NVS) and a variety of datasets. The paper demonstrates a single model tackling a broad range of scenarios without requiring task-specific fine-tuning or architectural changes. This is a significant step towards a more practical and deployable NVS system.
    *   **Video Quality:** The ability to generate long, temporally coherent videos with precise camera control and loop closure is a noteworthy achievement. This significantly elevates the quality of synthesized videos compared to prior approaches that often struggle with flickering or inconsistent results.
    *   **No NeRF Distillation:** The fact that SEVA doesn't require NeRF distillation simplifies the pipeline and makes it easier to apply in real-world scenarios.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The benchmark is extensive, including a wide range of datasets, metrics, and comparisons to both open-source and proprietary models. This provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of SEVA.
    *   **High-Quality Results:**  The visual results are compelling, showcasing photorealism, temporal smoothness, and 3D consistency.
    *   **Practicality:** The model's ability to handle variable input and target views and generate long videos without needing NeRF distillation increases its practicality for real-world applications.
    *   **Open-Source Release:** Releasing the model weights enables further research and development in the field.
* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Architecture:** The architectural improvements over prior diffusion models aren't groundbreaking.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models, in general, can be computationally expensive for inference. The paper doesn't extensively address the computational efficiency of SEVA.
    *   **Potential limitations regarding human/animal input/dynamic textures:** As the limitations are not directly tested against competing state of the art, there may be unforeseen issues related to such inputs.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper demonstrates significant progress in the field of novel view synthesis by presenting a versatile and high-performing model. While the architectural innovations are incremental, the carefully designed training and sampling strategies, combined with a comprehensive evaluation, make a substantial contribution. The model's ability to generalize across different tasks and datasets, generate long coherent videos, and avoid the need for NeRF distillation are significant advantages.

Taking into account these factors, I give the paper a score of **7.5**. The incremental architectural changes and limitations are countered by the high performance compared to prior work and the practical value of the generalisation.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deeply Supervised Flow-Based Generative Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14494v1)
- **Authors**: Inkyu Shin, Chenglin Yang, Liang-Chieh Chen
- **Abstract**: Flow based generative models have charted an impressive path across multiple visual generation tasks by adhering to a simple principle: learning velocity representations of a linear interpolant. However, we observe that training velocity solely from the final layer output underutilizes the rich inter layer representations, potentially impeding model convergence. To address this limitation, we introduce DeepFlow, a novel framework that enhances velocity representation through inter layer communication. DeepFlow partitions transformer layers into balanced branches with deep supervision and inserts a lightweight Velocity Refiner with Acceleration (VeRA) block between adjacent branches, which aligns the intermediate velocity features within transformer blocks. Powered by the improved deep supervision via the internal velocity alignment, DeepFlow converges 8 times faster on ImageNet with equivalent performance and further reduces FID by 2.6 while halving training time compared to previous flow based models without a classifier free guidance. DeepFlow also outperforms baselines in text to image generation tasks, as evidenced by evaluations on MSCOCO and zero shot GenEval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Deeply Supervised Flow-Based Generative Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepFlow, a novel framework to improve flow-based generative models by addressing limitations in how they learn velocity representations.  DeepFlow enhances velocity representations via inter-layer communication. It partitions transformer layers into branches with deep supervision and inserts a Velocity Refiner with Acceleration (VeRA) block between branches to align intermediate velocity features.  Experiments show DeepFlow leads to faster convergence, improved image generation quality (lower FID), and competitive performance on text-to-image generation, without relying on external models (unlike some prior works).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the DeepFlow framework's method for aligning intermediate velocity representations within a flow-based model.  While deep supervision and using intermediate layers aren't entirely new concepts in generative models, the specific approach of partitioning transformer blocks, the VeRA block designed to model acceleration between branches, and the specific application to flow-based models appear to be a novel combination.  The claim of internally self-correcting feature misalignment within transformer layers (as opposed to using external pre-trained models) is also a significant contribution.
    *   *Weakness:* The individual components like deep supervision and potentially similar types of feature alignment have been explored in other contexts. The novelty lies in the specific architectural choices and their interaction in a flow-based setup.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance is in improving the training efficiency and performance of flow-based generative models. If DeepFlow allows models to converge faster and achieve better image quality without needing external self-supervised models, it could make flow-based models more practical for a wider range of applications. The method addresses a perceived under-utilization of inter-layer information in existing flow-based models, indicating a clear understanding of limitations in the field and a targeted solution. Outperforming a strong baseline (SiT) is a good sign.
    *   *Weakness:* The results are primarily demonstrated on ImageNet and MS-COCO.  It would be stronger with a more extensive set of diverse datasets. While the reported FID improvements are significant, further analysis could explore whether these improvements translate into real-world advantages beyond standard benchmarks (e.g., diversity, controllability, or editability of generated images).

*   **Clarity and Evaluation:** The paper is well-written and presents its methods and results clearly. The ablation studies are crucial and effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component of DeepFlow. Quantitative results (FID, IS) are clearly presented, along with qualitative examples. The comparison against other generative model families and state-of-the-art approaches strengthens the claims.
    *   *Weakness:* The main experiments involve a small range of DeepFlow configurations.  A wider range of DeepFlow architectures and hyperparameter settings would further clarify the method's robustness and generalizability.

*   **Justification:** DeepFlow leverages second-order ODE function and second-order dynamics which makes it novel. The experiments justify the novelty with increased performance. VeRA block consists of an acceleration learning mechanism, which in turn refines the velocity feature. This leads to significant closer feature alignment, which leads to better image generation. The paper is well written overall and the approach is very creative.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel framework (DeepFlow) for improving flow-based generative models by focusing on inter-layer velocity representation alignment. The core contribution – the architectural design choices like the VeRA block and deep supervision branch placement – offers a concrete and well-supported improvement over existing methods. The empirical results, showing faster convergence and enhanced image quality, are encouraging. However, the novelty, while significant in its specific application, builds upon existing concepts in generative modeling. The lack of more extensive dataset diversity and a deeper dive into the practical benefits of the generated images beyond benchmark scores slightly temper the overall significance. Nevertheless, the paper contributes a valuable approach with clear potential to influence the development of more efficient and effective flow-based generative models.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Power of Context: How Multimodality Improves Image Super-Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14503v1)
- **Authors**: Kangfu Mei, Hossein Talebi, Mojtaba Ardakani, Vishal M. Patel, Peyman Milanfar, Mauricio Delbracio
- **Abstract**: Single-image super-resolution (SISR) remains challenging due to the inherent difficulty of recovering fine-grained details and preserving perceptual quality from low-resolution inputs. Existing methods often rely on limited image priors, leading to suboptimal results. We propose a novel approach that leverages the rich contextual information available in multiple modalities -- including depth, segmentation, edges, and text prompts -- to learn a powerful generative prior for SISR within a diffusion model framework. We introduce a flexible network architecture that effectively fuses multimodal information, accommodating an arbitrary number of input modalities without requiring significant modifications to the diffusion process. Crucially, we mitigate hallucinations, often introduced by text prompts, by using spatial information from other modalities to guide regional text-based conditioning. Each modality's guidance strength can also be controlled independently, allowing steering outputs toward different directions, such as increasing bokeh through depth or adjusting object prominence via segmentation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our model surpasses state-of-the-art generative SISR methods, achieving superior visual quality and fidelity. See project page at https://mmsr.kfmei.com/.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and a justified novelty score for this paper.

**Summary**

The paper "The Power of Context: How Multimodality Improves Image Super-Resolution" introduces a novel approach to single-image super-resolution (SISR) by incorporating multiple modalities of contextual information.  Instead of relying solely on the low-resolution input image, the method leverages depth maps, semantic segmentation maps, edge information, and text prompts (captions) to guide the super-resolution process.  The core of the approach involves a diffusion model conditioned on these various modalities, fused through a multimodal latent connector (MMLC). This is designed to efficiently integrate the information from these different sources.  The method also introduces a multimodal classifier-free guidance technique to mitigate hallucinations and enables independent control of each modality's influence. The authors present experimental results demonstrating that their approach surpasses state-of-the-art generative SISR methods in terms of visual quality and fidelity.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper does present some novel aspects, but the overall level of innovation could be higher. The core idea of using multiple modalities is not entirely new in image restoration; many prior works use depth or segmentation. However, the *specific combination* of modalities (depth, segmentation, edges, captions), the design of a network to efficiently fuse them (the MMLC), and the multimodal classifier-free guidance are contributions. While the paper leverages existing VQGAN and diffusion model architectures, it offers a specific adaptation and fusion strategy for SISR. The architecture innovations like the multi-modal latent connector and the token-wise encoding are the paper's strongest novelty aspects.

*   **Significance:** SISR is a well-established and important problem in computer vision. Improving the realism and fidelity of super-resolved images has many applications. The paper's ability to generate images that not only achieve good quantitative metrics but also exhibit superior visual quality and avoid artifacts inconsistent with the low-resolution input is significant. It offers a path toward more reliable and controllable SISR. The ability to manipulate the influence of different modalities has the potential to be very useful for downstream tasks.
    The paper's results are strong, but are they transformative? Perhaps not. The improvement seems incremental but meaningful compared to existing generative SISR techniques.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Effective Multimodal Fusion:** The MMLC appears to be a well-designed component for efficiently integrating diverse modalities. The token-wise encoding to inject the modalities is also an elegant solution.
    *   **Controlled Generation:** The ability to adjust the influence of each modality provides a means to steer the generation process and explore different artistic or application-specific styles.
    *   **Hallucination Mitigation:** The multimodal classifier-free guidance is a valuable contribution, addressing a common problem in generative SISR.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides solid evidence that the proposed method outperforms state-of-the-art techniques on multiple benchmark datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The improvement over existing methods, while significant, isn't necessarily groundbreaking. The core relies on pre-trained diffusion models, with most novelty coming from the input modalities.
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:**  The method relies on pre-trained models for modality extraction (depth, segmentation, captioning). The overall performance depends on the quality of these individual modules, and errors from these models could propagate through the system.
    *  **Computational Cost:** Although the multi-modal latent connector is designed for efficiency, the use of multi-modal data increases the memory requirement.
    *   **Generalizability:**  The specific modalities chosen are somewhat arbitrary. A discussion regarding the selection and generalization to different data types, or tasks that have access to different auxiliary data would have been useful.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** While the paper includes ablation studies, it could benefit from more analysis of the impact of individual modality combinations. For instance, what modalities are most important for what kind of scene?

**Justification for Score**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a well-executed approach to SISR that effectively incorporates multimodal contextual information. The novelty lies in the specific fusion strategy (MMLC), the multimodal classifier-free guidance, and the combination of modalities used. The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over state-of-the-art methods, suggesting a practical impact.
However, the reliance on pre-trained models, the incremental nature of the improvements, and the limited analysis of specific modality contributions prevent a higher score. While the paper pushes the boundaries of SISR, it doesn't represent a fundamental paradigm shift. The use of diffusion models is also not novel in itself, just the application to the multimodal guidance problem. This score indicates that the paper contains significant contributions and represents a useful advance in the field but doesn't necessarily revolutionize SISR.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Aligning Multimodal LLM with Human Preference: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14504v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Yu, Yi-Fan Zhang, Chaoyou Fu, Junkang Wu, Jinda Lu, Kun Wang, Xingyu Lu, Yunhang Shen, Guibin Zhang, Dingjie Song, Yibo Yan, Tianlong Xu, Qingsong Wen, Zhang Zhang, Yan Huang, Liang Wang, Tieniu Tan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) can handle a wide variety of general tasks with simple prompts, without the need for task-specific training. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), built upon LLMs, have demonstrated impressive potential in tackling complex tasks involving visual, auditory, and textual data. However, critical issues related to truthfulness, safety, o1-like reasoning, and alignment with human preference remain insufficiently addressed. This gap has spurred the emergence of various alignment algorithms, each targeting different application scenarios and optimization goals. Recent studies have shown that alignment algorithms are a powerful approach to resolving the aforementioned challenges. In this paper, we aim to provide a comprehensive and systematic review of alignment algorithms for MLLMs. Specifically, we explore four key aspects: (1) the application scenarios covered by alignment algorithms, including general image understanding, multi-image, video, and audio, and extended multimodal applications; (2) the core factors in constructing alignment datasets, including data sources, model responses, and preference annotations; (3) the benchmarks used to evaluate alignment algorithms; and (4) a discussion of potential future directions for the development of alignment algorithms. This work seeks to help researchers organize current advancements in the field and inspire better alignment methods. The project page of this paper is available at https://github.com/BradyFU/Awesome-Multimodal-Large-Language-Models/tree/Alignment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper draft:

**Summary:**

The paper is a survey on alignment methods for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It systematically reviews existing alignment algorithms, focusing on four key aspects: (1) the application scenarios the algorithms address (e.g., general image understanding, video, audio); (2) the construction of alignment datasets (data sources, model responses, and preference annotations); (3) the benchmarks used for evaluation; and (4) potential future directions in the field. The survey provides a taxonomy of alignment approaches, discusses the challenges in creating high-quality alignment data, and organizes benchmarks based on the aspects of model behavior they assess (e.g., hallucination, safety). The paper also identifies areas where future research is needed, such as better leveraging visual information during alignment, incorporating insights from LLM alignment, and addressing security concerns in MLLM agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty of this paper lies in its focus specifically on *MLLM* alignment.  While existing surveys address AI alignment more broadly, this paper carves out a niche by concentrating on the unique challenges and techniques applied to models that process multiple modalities. The paper effectively synthesizes a rapidly growing body of research. The framework provided for categorizing application scenarios and the analysis of dataset construction are valuable contributions. The identification of future research directions adds to the paper's value. However, the *algorithms themselves are not novel*. The paper's value is in *organizing and analyzing* existing algorithms within a specific context.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the increasing importance of MLLMs. Alignment is a crucial step in ensuring that these models are truthful, safe, and aligned with human preferences. The survey provides a much-needed roadmap for researchers and practitioners working in this area. By identifying key challenges and future directions, the paper can help guide future research efforts and accelerate progress in MLLM alignment. The comprehensive overview, the unified notation (referenced in the text, not available in the snippets provided), and the categorization of benchmarks offer practical value to the community. The github repo alone significantly contributes.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Scope:** The paper clearly defines its focus and scope, making it easy for the reader to understand the topic.
*   **Systematic Approach:** The systematic review of alignment algorithms, datasets, and benchmarks provides a well-organized overview of the field.
*   **Comprehensive Coverage:**  The paper covers a wide range of relevant research, demonstrating a thorough understanding of the current state-of-the-art.
*   **Practical Value:** The taxonomy of application scenarios, the analysis of dataset construction, and the categorization of benchmarks provide practical guidance for researchers and practitioners.
*   **Future Directions:** The identification of future research directions can help guide future research efforts and accelerate progress in the field.
*   **Github repo:** The existence of a github repo for the project significantly improves the paper's impact by allowing future researchers to follow along and easily understand the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited technical depth on individual algorithms:**  While the paper surveys many algorithms, it doesn't delve deeply into the technical details of each one. It primarily offers a high-level overview. This can be expected in a survey.
*   **Dependency on existing research:** By its nature, a survey is dependent on existing research. The originality comes from the structure given to the research, and the analysis and opinions of the researchers.
*   **The constant influx of new research:** The MLLM landscape evolves very quickly, and the usefulness of a survey degrades as time passes. This is a problem with all modern-AI surveys.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by organizing and synthesizing the rapidly growing field of MLLM alignment. The focus on MLLMs is timely and relevant, and the systematic review provides a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners. The paper excels in providing a cohesive framework, analyzing dataset construction challenges, and identifying promising research avenues. This paper provides the first such survey of MLLMs.

However, the paper's lack of deep technical analysis of individual algorithms and its reliance on existing research limit its novelty. While the survey is comprehensive, it does not present entirely novel algorithmic insights. The field is also evolving so rapidly that this paper runs the risk of quickly becoming outdated.

Considering these factors, a score of **7** is appropriate. It is a well-executed survey with significant practical value, but it is not a groundbreaking contribution that fundamentally alters the landscape of MLLM research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MusicInfuser: Making Video Diffusion Listen and Dance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14505v1)
- **Authors**: Susung Hong, Ira Kemelmacher-Shlizerman, Brian Curless, Steven M. Seitz
- **Abstract**: We introduce MusicInfuser, an approach for generating high-quality dance videos that are synchronized to a specified music track. Rather than attempting to design and train a new multimodal audio-video model, we show how existing video diffusion models can be adapted to align with musical inputs by introducing lightweight music-video cross-attention and a low-rank adapter. Unlike prior work requiring motion capture data, our approach fine-tunes only on dance videos. MusicInfuser achieves high-quality music-driven video generation while preserving the flexibility and generative capabilities of the underlying models. We introduce an evaluation framework using Video-LLMs to assess multiple dimensions of dance generation quality. The project page and code are available at https://susunghong.github.io/MusicInfuser.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "MusicInfuser: Making Video Diffusion Listen and Dance" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MusicInfuser, a novel approach to generate high-quality dance videos synchronized to a given music track. Instead of training a new, complex multimodal audio-video model from scratch, MusicInfuser adapts existing pre-trained text-to-video diffusion models. This adaptation is achieved by introducing lightweight music-video cross-attention and a low-rank adapter. The system is trained exclusively on dance videos, avoiding the need for motion capture data. MusicInfuser allows users to control dance style and setting through text prompts while maintaining synchronization with the music. The authors introduce an evaluation framework using Video-LLMs to assess dance generation quality.  Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of MusicInfuser in generating music-driven dance videos while preserving the flexibility and generative capabilities of the underlying video diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the **adaptive approach** of modifying existing diffusion models rather than creating a new architecture. While cross-attention mechanisms and LoRA are individually known, their combined and targeted application within a text-to-video diffusion model for music synchronization represents a valuable contribution. Critically, the **zero-initialization strategy** for the cross-attention layers is a clever way to preserve the original diffusion model's knowledge and prevent disruption during initial training. The **beta-uniform scheduling** also adds a layer of fine-grained control over the training process. The use of only dance videos for fine-tuning, as opposed to motion capture, is a significant practical advantage.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem: generating synchronized audio-visual content. The focus on dance is a specific and well-defined instantiation of this more general challenge. The fact that MusicInfuser can be controlled through text prompts is highly desirable, as it makes the system more accessible and versatile. The demonstrated ability to generate group choreography and longer videos further enhances the significance. The introduction of the Video-LLM based evaluation framework contributes to the assessment of generated content and provides a benchmark for future work. The demonstration on music not seen during training enhances the significance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The adaptive approach reduces the need for large, specialized datasets and training infrastructure.
    *   The text-prompt control offers flexibility and creativity.
    *   The introduction of the Video-LLM evaluation framework provides a standardized way to measure performance.
    *   Good quality of results relative to the prior art shown in figure 5.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the authors demonstrate qualitative results, the quality of the movements, especially in complex choreographies or unseen styles, may vary significantly.
    *   The method inherits limitations from the underlying diffusion models, such as potential artifacts or inconsistencies in generated content, especially in fine parts such as fingers and faces. The model also sometimes struggles with silhouettes.
    *   While a custom evaluation framework is presented, it is still based on a subjective, prompt-guided assessment. A more objective, quantifiable metric (e.g., analyzing the frequency spectrum of generated movements) could strengthen the evaluation.
    *   The experiments could be extended by making a human preference judgement between MusicInfuser results vs. applying post-processing to existing video generation methods (prompt editing and music synchronization).

**Justification:**

While the paper builds on existing techniques, the *specific combination* of adaptive fine-tuning, zero-initialization, beta scheduling, and a Video-LLM evaluation makes the work more than incremental. The ability to synchronize dance videos to music *without* motion capture data is a significant advantage. The overall framework addresses an important challenge in audio-visual content creation. Given the limitations discussed (inherited diffusion model weaknesses, potential for subjectivity in the evaluation), a rating is justified. The quantitative improvements shown (Table 1 and 2) give a good reason for significance.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
